\alpha\leq\sqrt{\dfrac{2m-1}{2}},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2m-1}}.

(2.68)

Therefore, thanks to (
2.64
), (
2.66
), and (
2.68
), provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\max\left\{\frac{5}{\sqrt{2}\,m},\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{m},\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2m-1}}\right\}=\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2m-1}},

(2.69)

we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)=\left(4m^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

The proof of the theorem also shows that the spectrum of
\displaystyle\Pi_{n^{2}}(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m})
is given by

\displaystyle\lambda=\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\theta_{k}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\theta_{k}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

where
\left\{\theta_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathbb{Z}
is the discrete sets of zeroes of the function

\displaystyle\psi(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(\left(1+e^{2\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2})\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\theta^{2}}}\right)\cos(\theta)-2\,e^{\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2})\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\theta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\theta\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2})\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\theta^{2}}-(m^{2}+n^{2})\left(e^{2\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2})\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\theta^{2}}}-1\right)\sin(\theta).

By parity, we have
\theta_{-k}=\theta_{k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Indeed, looking at (
2.7
), we deduce that for
h_{1}(\theta)>0
for
\theta
large enough. Since the dominant term of
h_{1}
is
\theta^{2}e^{\sqrt{a^{2}+\theta^{2}}}
which is also the dominant term of
h_{2}
(while
h_{3}
is negligible), we deduce that
\psi^{\prime}
changed of sign infinitely often. Since (using (
2.51
))

\displaystyle\psi(2k\pi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(e^{\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2}){\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+(2k\pi)^{2}}}-1\right)^{2}2k\pi\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2}){\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+(2k\pi)^{2}}>0

\displaystyle\psi((2k+1)\pi)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(e^{\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2}){\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+(2k+1)^{2}\pi^{2}}}+1\right)^{2}(2k+1)\pi

\displaystyle\times\sqrt{(2m^{2}+2n^{2}){\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+(2k+1)^{2}\pi^{2}}<0,

we deduce that
\psi
admits a discrete set of zeroes
\left\{\theta_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
\theta_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\pm\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\pm\infty
. Furthermore, the previous analysis suggests that for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle k\,\pi<\theta_{k}<(k+1)\pi.

(2.70)

Let us also mention that numerical experiments suggest that

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(a,b)\underset{\frac{b}{a}\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\pi.

(2.71)

Indeed, taking
m=n=1
, and
R^{2}=10000
, a numerical estimation gives
0<\theta_{1}-\pi<2\cdot 10^{-88}
and
-2\cdot 10^{-88}<\theta_{2}-2\pi<0
, which also suggests more generally that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\theta_{k}(a,b)\underset{\frac{b}{a}\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}k\,\pi,

(2.72)

but that the inequality (
2.70
) is no longer true (provided that
|k|\geq 2
) when the conformal class is large enough.

Theorem 2.7
.

Let
m\geq 1
, and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2m-1}}.

(2.73)

Then, if
\lambda_{m}>0
is defined by (
2.9
), we have the estimate

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}>\left(4m^{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

(2.74)

Furthermore, provided that
m\geq 1
is an integer and

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{1}{2m-1}\sqrt{12m^{2}+4m-1+\sqrt{(12m^{2}+4m-1)^{2}+60(2m-1)^{2}}},

(2.75)

then

\displaystyle\left(4m^{2}\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}<\lambda_{m}<\left(4m^{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

(2.76)

and the minimiser is unique and given by

\displaystyle u(r,\theta)=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(u_{m}(r)e^{i\,m\,\theta}\right),

where
Y(t)=u_{m}(\log(t))\in W^{2,2}_{0}([\log(a),\log(b)])
is a non-trivial solution to the following ordinary differential equation

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(t)-4\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)-2(2m^{2}-3)Y^{\prime\prime}(t)+4(2m^{2}-1)Y^{\prime}(t)-\left(\lambda_{m}-\left(2m^{2}(m^{2}-2)+1\right)\right)Y(t)=0,

where
\lambda_{m}>0
is as above.

Proof.

The hypothesis implies in particular that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{5}{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}}}.

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\frac{5}{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}}}\leq\frac{5}{m\sqrt{2}}<\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{m}

as
\pi>5/2
.
Therefore, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, thanks to Theorem
2.5
, there exists a solution
Y_{n}
to the linear differential equation (
2.5
) with boundary conditions (
2.34
), with minimal
\lambda_{m,n}>0
that satisfies the bound of Theorem
2.5
. Therefore, if
f_{n}(r)=Y_{n}(\log(r))
, we deduce that
f_{n}
solves the equation (
2.6
), and

\displaystyle v_{n}(r,\theta)=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(f_{n}(r)e^{i\,n\,\theta}\right)

that we scale (without changing notations) so that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{v_{n}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=1,

is an admissible minimiser of (
2.9
) with

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}v_{n}\right)^{2}dx=\lambda_{m,n}.

(2.77)

Now, let
u
be a minimiser of of (
2.9
). Writing

\displaystyle u(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}u_{n}(r)e^{i\,n\,\theta}

Since
\lambda_{m}>0
, we have
u\neq 0
, and we deduce that there exists
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
u_{n}\neq 0
. In particular, we deduce by Theorem
2.5
since
Y(t)=u_{n}(e^{t})
is a non-trivial solution of (
2.5
) that

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}\geq\lambda_{m,n}>\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

By the proof of Theorem
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)=\left(4m^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}

thanks to our hypothesis on
R=\log\left(\dfrac{b}{a}\right)
, the bound of the theorem is proved. Now, we need only prove that
\lambda_{m}=\lambda_{m,m}
provided that the conformal class is large enough.

By contradiction, if
\lambda_{m}>\lambda_{m,m}
, then
v_{m}
is a solution that has a strictly smaller energy by (
2.77
). Now, assume that there exists
n\neq m
such that
u_{n}\neq 0
. Then, we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{m}\geq\lambda_{m,n}>\lambda_{m,m},

provided that
R
is big enough, thanks to the estimate

\displaystyle\lambda_{m,n}<\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Indeed, we have
\lambda_{m,m}<\lambda_{m,n}
provided that

\displaystyle\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\geq\left(4m^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}

(2.78)

for some integer
n\neq m
. Since the left-hand side is symmetric in
n
, letting
\alpha=\dfrac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, making the change of variable
n=m+1+t
and
n=m-1-s
we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((m-n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m+n)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\inf_{t\geq 0}\left((t+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m+1+t)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right),\inf_{s\geq 0}\left((s+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m-1-s)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\right\}.

Therefore, we introduce the following function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}

\displaystyle f(t)

\displaystyle=\left((t+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m+1+t)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right).

As
f
is trivially increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{t\geq 0}f(t)=f(0)=\left(1+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right).

Likewise, let
g:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be such that

\displaystyle g(t)=\left(t^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m-t)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)

that we minimise on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\cap\left\{t:t\geq 1\right\}
. We have

\displaystyle g(t)=\left(t^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left(t^{2}-4m\,t+4m^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)=t^{4}-4m\,t^{3}+2(2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})t^{2}-4m\alpha^{2}t+\alpha^{2}(4m^{2}+\alpha^{2}).

Since
g
is coercive, it admits a minimum.
We have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=4t^{3}-12mt^{2}+4(2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})t-4m\alpha^{2}=4(t^{3}-3mt^{2}+(2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})t-m\alpha^{2}).

We immediately see that
t=m
is a root of
g^{\prime}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)=4(t-m)(t^{2}-2mt+\alpha^{2})=4(t-m)((t-m)^{2}-(m^{2}-\alpha^{2})).

Assuming from now on that
\alpha^{2}\leq m^{2}
, we deduce that
(t-m)^{2}-(m^{2}-\alpha^{2})\geq 0
if and only if

\displaystyle t\leq m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}\quad\text{or}\;\,t\geq m+\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}.

Therefore, we deduce that
g
is decreasing on
]-\infty,m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}]
, increasing on
[m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}},m]
, decreasing on
[m,m+\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}]
, and increasing on
[m+\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}},\infty[
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\mathbb{R}}g(t)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{g(m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}),g(m+\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}})\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left(\left(m+\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}\right)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right).

Now, since we look for the minimum of
g
on
\mathbb{Z}\setminus\left\{m\right\}
, notice that
m-\sqrt{m^{2}-\alpha^{2}}\leq 1
if and only if

\displaystyle m\geq\frac{1+\alpha^{2}}{2}

(2.79)

and we finally deduce that whenever the bound (
2.79
) is satisfied (notice that it is satisfied by hypothesis), we have

\displaystyle\inf_{t\geq 1}g(t)=g(1)=\left(1+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m-1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right).

Therefore thanks to (
2.78
), we have
\lambda_{m,m}<\lambda_{m,n}
for all
n\neq m
provided that

\displaystyle\left(1+\alpha^{2}\right)\left((2m-1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)>4\alpha^{2}(4m^{2}+4\alpha^{2}),

or

\displaystyle 15\alpha^{4}+(12m^{2}+4m-1)\alpha^{2}-(2m-1)^{2}\leq 0;

which yields the condition

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{30}\left(-(12m^{2}+4m-1)+\sqrt{(12m^{2}+4m-1)^{2}+60(2m-1)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(2m-1)^{2}}{12m^{2}+4m-1+\sqrt{(12m^{2}+4m-1)^{2}+60(2m-1)^{2}}}.

which finally furnishes the condition given in the statement of the theorem.
∎

3
Second Eigenvalue Problem

The first ODE lemma will allow us (together with the forthcoming weighted Poincaré estimates) to control terms of the form

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|A|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

in the second derivative of the Willmore energy. However, the terms of the form

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|A|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

require a new estimate of the form

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\mathscr{L}_{m}u)^{2}dx\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\quad\forall u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega),

where
\lambda_{0}>0
is independent of
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. The method to prove such an identity is now clear.

3.1
Minimisation Problem

Fix
0<a<b<\infty
, and consider the following minimisation problem

\displaystyle\mu_{m}=\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\mathscr{L}_{m}u)^{2}dx:u\in W^{2,2}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=1\right\}.

(3.1)

A minimiser
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
exists thanks to a similar proof, and it verifies

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u=-\frac{\mu_{m}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\Delta-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\right)u.

(3.2)

Indeed, integrating by parts, for all
u,v\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla u\cdot\nabla v}{|x|^{2}}dx=-\int_{\Omega}v\dive\left(\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\nabla u\right)dx=-\int_{\Omega}v\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left(\Delta u-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)dx.

Notice that we trivially have for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\Pi_{n^{2}}\left(\Delta-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\right)=\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{n^{2}}{r^{2}}.

Recalling formula (
2.4
), if

\displaystyle\Pi_{n^{2}}(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m})=\partial_{r}^{4}+\frac{2}{r}\partial_{r}^{3}-(2(m+1)(m-1)+2\,n^{2}+1)\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}+(2(m+1)(m-1)+2\,n^{2}+1)\frac{1}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}

\displaystyle+\left((m+1)^{2}(m-1)^{2}-2n^{2}(m+1)(m-1)+n^{2}(n^{2}-4)\right)\frac{1}{r^{4}},

we deduce that for all
\mu\geq 0

\displaystyle\Pi_{n^{2}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}+\frac{\mu}{|x|^{2}}\left(\Delta-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\right)\right)=\partial_{r}^{4}+\frac{2}{r}\partial_{r}^{3}-(2(m^{2}-1)+2\,n^{2}+1)\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle+(2(m^{2}-1)+2\,n^{2}+1)\frac{1}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}+\left((m^{2}-1)^{2}-2n^{2}(m^{2}-1)+n^{2}(n^{2}-4)\right)\frac{1}{r^{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu}{r^{2}}\left(\Delta-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}-\frac{n^{2}}{r^{2}}\right).

Now, consider a smooth solution to the equation

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left(2m^{2}+2n^{2}-1\right)\frac{1}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}+\left(2m^{2}+2n^{2}-1\right)\frac{1}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}+\left((m^{2}-n^{2}-1)^{2}-4n^{2})\right)\frac{1}{r^{4}}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mu}{r^{2}}\left(f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{1}{r}f^{\prime}-\frac{n^{2}}{r^{2}}f\right).

(3.3)

Making as previously the change of variable
f(r)=Y(\log(r))
, remember that by (
2.27
) and (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{r}f(r)=\frac{1}{r}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{2}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{3}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{3}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{2}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{4}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{11}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r)).\end{aligned}\right.

(3.4)

Therefore, (
2.6
) and (
2.27
) show that

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{2}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-(2m^{2}+2n^{2}-1)\frac{1}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}+(2m^{2}+2n^{2}-1)\frac{1}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{4}}\bigg(Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-4\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-2(m^{2}+n^{2}-3)Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))+4(m^{2}+n^{2}-1)Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\bigg).

(3.5)

On the other hand (
3.4
) shows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{1}{r}f^{\prime}-\frac{n^{2}}{r^{2}}f\right)=\frac{1}{r^{4}}\bigg(Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-2\,Y^{\prime}(\log(r))-n^{2}Y(\log(r))\bigg),

(3.6)

and taking
r=e^{t}
, we deduce that (
3.1
) is equivalent to the equation

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(t)-4\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)-\left(2(m^{2}+n^{2}-3)-\mu\right)Y^{\prime\prime}(t)+(4(m^{2}+n^{2}-1)-\mu)Y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle+\left((m^{2}-n^{2}-1)^{2}-(4+\mu)n^{2}\right)Y(t)=0.

(3.7)

The characteristic polynomial of this equation is given by

\displaystyle P(X)=X^{4}-4\,X^{3}-(2(m^{2}+n^{2}-3)-\mu)X^{2}+2(2(m^{2}+n^{2}-1)-\mu)X+(m^{2}-n^{2}-1)^{2}-(4+\mu)n^{2}.

First consider the polynomial

\displaystyle R(X)=X^{2}-2X-n^{2}.

We have

\displaystyle R(Y+1)=Y^{2}-n^{2}-1.

Therefore, the computation in (
2.6
) shows that

\displaystyle Q(Y)

\displaystyle=P(Y+1)=Y^{4}-2(m^{2}+n^{2})Y^{2}+(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}+\mu\left(Y^{2}-n^{2}-1\right)

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-(2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu)Y^{2}+(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}-\mu(n^{2}+1)

which is once more a biquadratic polynomial! Therefore, the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=1+\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}}\\
r_{2}&=1-\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}}\\
r_{3}&=1+\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu-\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}}\\
r_{4}&=1-\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu-\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}}.\end{aligned}\right.

We will distinguish three cases provided that
n\geq 1
or
m=1
and
n\geq 0
. Provided that either of those conditions hold, we deduce that
16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}\geq 0
, and we have

\displaystyle 2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu-\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}\leq 0

if and only if

\displaystyle 4(m^{2}+n^{2})^{2}-4(m^{2}+n^{2})\mu+\mu^{2}\leq 16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2},

or

\displaystyle\mu\geq\frac{1}{n^{2}+1}\left((m^{2}+n^{2})^{2}-4m^{2}n^{2}\right)=\frac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}.

3.2
General ODE Lemma

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
m\geq 1
, and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Let
0<a<b<\infty
and distinguish the following two cases.

Assumption I:
n^{2}>\dfrac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}}
. Then, we assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\pi\sqrt{\frac{25(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}}{2(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}}.

Assumption II:
m\geq\sqrt{2}
and
n^{2}\leq\dfrac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}}
. Then, we make no assumptions on
0<a<b<\infty
.

For all
\lambda>0
, consider the following linear differential equation

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(t)-4\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)-\left(2(m^{2}+n^{2}-3)-\mu\right)Y^{\prime\prime}(t)+(4(m^{2}+n^{2}-1)-\mu)Y^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle+\left((m^{2}-n^{2}-1)^{2}-(4+\mu)n^{2}\right)Y(t)=0

(3.8)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} Y(\log(a))&=Y(\log(b))&&=0\\
Y^{\prime}(\log(a))&=Y^{\prime}(\log(b))&&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.9)

Then, there exists
\mu>0
such that the system (
3.1
)-(
3.9
) admits a non-trivial solution
Y
. Furthermore, the minimal value
\mu_{m,n}>0
satisfies the following estimate

\displaystyle\frac{\left(2(m+n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)}{4(n^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}<\mu_{m,n}<\frac{\left((m+n)^{2}+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m-n)^{2}+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)}{n^{2}+1+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(3.10)

provided that
Assumption I
or
Assumption II
hold. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{m,n}>\frac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}>\frac{m^{4}}{4}\qquad\text{if}\quad n^{2}\leq\frac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}}

if
Assumption III
holds.
Finally, assuming that (
3.25
), (
3.26
)
(
if
m>1
)
, and (
3.27
)
(
if
m\geq r_{0}
, where
8<r_{0}<9
is the largest root of the polynomial
X^{4}-8X^{3}-6X^{2}+1
)
, we have
we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{m,n}>\frac{\left(4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}},

(3.11)

while the conditions (
3.25
), (
3.26
)
(
if
m>1
)
, (
3.27
)
(
if
m\geq r_{0}
, where
8<r_{0}<9
is the largest root of the polynomial
X^{4}-8X^{3}-6X^{2}+1
)
, and (
3.30
) imply that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{m,n}=\mu_{m,m}>\frac{\left(4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(3.12)

Proof.

Assumption I:
n\geq 1
or
m=1
.

Without loss of generality, since we are interested in the asymptotic behaviour of the first eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}(\Omega)
as
\dfrac{b}{a}\rightarrow\infty
, which we know converges to
0
, we can assume that
\mu<2(m^{2}+n^{2})
(more precisely, we will show that the minimal solution is such that
\mu<2(m^{2}+n^{2})
, which shows that this hypothesis is not restrictive)..

Let
Y
be a non-trivial solution of the equation (
3.1
) such that
\mu>0
. We distinguish three cases.

Case 1:

\mu<\dfrac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}
.

Then, the characteristic polynomial
P
admits four distinct real roots, which shows that for some
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3},\mu_{4})\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{r_{1}\,t}+\mu_{2}e^{r_{2}\,t}+\mu_{3}e^{r_{3}\,t}+\mu_{4}e^{r_{4}\,t},

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}=1+\lambda_{1}\\
r_{2}=1-\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}=1+\lambda_{2}\\
r_{4}=1-\lambda_{2}\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda_{1}&=\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}}\\
\lambda_{2}&=\sqrt{\frac{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu-\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}}{2}},\end{aligned}\right.

we are exactly in the situation of the first step in the proof of Theorem
2.5
, and the boundary conditions imply that

\displaystyle a^{2}b^{2}\left((\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})^{2}\left(\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}\right)-\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)^{2}\left(\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)-8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\right)=0,

which is impossible since
\lambda_{1}>\lambda_{2}>0
. Therefore, we get
Y=0
, a contradiction.

Case 2:

\mu=\dfrac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}
. Then, provided that
\mu\leq 2(m^{2}+n^{2})
we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=1+\sqrt{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu}=\sqrt{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\frac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}}\\
r_{2}&=1-\sqrt{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu}\\
r_{3}&=r_{4}=1\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, we deduce one more by the proof of Theorem
2.5
(one need only replace
2m^{2}+2n^{2}
by
2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu
in formulae) that

\displaystyle\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}\,(ab)^{2}\left(4-2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}}-2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}}-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0.

(3.13)

Since
\mu<2(m^{2}+n^{2})
by hypothesis, we deduce that (
3.2
) is never verified, which implies that
Y=0
.

Case 3:

\mu>\dfrac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}
. Then, the characteristic polynomial admits the following roots

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}=1+\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu}{2}}\\
r_{2}=1-\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu}{2}}\\
r_{3}=1+i\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+\mu-2(m^{2}+n^{2})}{2}}\\
r_{4}=1-i\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+\mu-2(m^{2}+n^{2})}{2}}\end{aligned}\right.

We are exactly in the situation of
Step 3
from Theorem (
2.5
), and we deduce by (
2.47
) also holds, namely

\displaystyle 2\left(\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}-\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0.

(3.14)

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda_{1}&=\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu}{2}}\\
\lambda_{2}&=\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+\mu-2(m^{2}+n^{2})}{2}}\end{aligned}\right.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{2}^{2}=2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu,

and we get the equation

\displaystyle\left(\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}=\left(m^{2}+n^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right),

(3.15)

where
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\lambda_{2}^{2}}
, and finally, we recover a variant of (
2.48
)

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}=\left(m^{2}+n^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}-1\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right),

(3.16)

where
2m^{2}+2n^{2}
is simply substituted to
2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu
. Therefore, using (
2.61
), provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{5}{\sqrt{2m^{2}+2n^{2}-\mu}},

(3.17)

the first non-trivial solution
\lambda_{2}>0
of this equation is such that

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}<\lambda_{2}<\frac{2\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

(3.18)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}+\mu-2(m^{2}+n^{2})}>\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

which gives us

\displaystyle\sqrt{16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}}>2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and

\displaystyle 16m^{2}n^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu+\mu^{2}>\left(2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)^{2},

that we rewrite

\displaystyle\mu^{2}-4(m^{2}-1)\mu>\left(2(m+n)^{2}-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

(3.19)

In order to simplify notations, write

\displaystyle X=\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

We have

\displaystyle\left(2(m+n)^{2}-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}-\mu+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(2(m+n)^{2}+X^{2}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}+X^{2}\right)-2\left(2(m^{2}+n^{2})+X^{2}\right)\mu+\mu^{2}

where we used the parallelogram identity
(m^{2}+n^{2})^{2}+(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}=2m^{4}+2n^{4}
. Therefore, (
3.19
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2\left(2(n^{2}+1)+X^{2}\right)\mu

\displaystyle=\left(2(2(m^{2}+n^{2})+X^{2})-4(m^{2}-1)\right)\mu

\displaystyle>\left(2(m+n)^{2}+X^{2}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}+X^{2}\right),

and we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mu>\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(2(m+n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)}{2(n^{2}+1)+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(3.20)

Furthermore, (
3.18
) also shows that

\displaystyle\mu<\frac{\left((m+n)^{2}+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((m-n)^{2}+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)}{n^{2}+1+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(3.21)

Step 4: Conformal class estimate.

Now, recall that (
3.17
) depends on
\mu
, but it should only depend on
m
and
n
. Therefore, we need to rewrite the condition so that it only depends on
m
and
n
. If
Y^{2}=2X^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle 2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\frac{\left((m+n)^{2}+Y^{2}\right)((m-n)^{2}+Y^{2})}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}\left(2(m^{2}+n^{2})(n^{2}+1+\cancel{Y^{2}})-\left((m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}+\cancel{2(m^{2}+n^{2})Y^{2}}+Y^{4}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}\left({-Y^{4}+2(m^{2}+n^{2})(n^{2}+1)-(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}\left(-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)\right).

(3.22)

That gives us a non-trivial condition on
Y
provided that
n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)>0
. The discriminant of this polynomial in
n^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle 4(2m^{2}+1)^{2}+4m^{2}(m^{2}-2)=4\left(5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1\right),

which shows that its roots are given by

\displaystyle-(2m^{2}+1)\pm\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}.

Sub-case 1:
1\leq m<\sqrt{2}
.

We have

\displaystyle 2(m^{2}+n^{2})-\frac{\left((m+n)^{2}+Y^{2}\right)\left((m-n)^{2}+Y^{2}\right)}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}\left(-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2}\right)

and we get the
necessary
condition

\displaystyle Y<\sqrt[4]{n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2}},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt[4]{n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2}}}.

Therefore, the condition (
3.17
) is implied by

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{Y}\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}{-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2}}}

or

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}(-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})>25Y^{2}\left(Y^{2}+1\right),

that we finally rewrite as

\displaystyle n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2}\geq\left(\frac{25}{2\pi^{2}}+1\right)Y^{4}+\frac{25}{2\pi^{2}}(n^{2}+1)Y^{2}.

Therefore, we finally get the condition

\displaystyle Y^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{25}{4\pi^{2}}(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{\frac{525}{16\pi^{4}}(n^{2}+1)^{2}+\left(\frac{50}{\pi^{2}}+4\right)(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{25}{4\pi^{2}}(n^{2}+1)+\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{4(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}{25(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}}

which can can rewritten as

\displaystyle Y^{2}=2X^{2}=\frac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}

as

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\pi\sqrt{\frac{25(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}}{2(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}+(2-m^{2})m^{2})}},

which concludes the proof of the theorem in the case
1\leq m<\sqrt{2}
. Notice that as
|n|\rightarrow\infty
, the right-hand side converges to
0
, which implies in particular that this quantity is bounded as
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Sub-case 2:
m\geq\sqrt{2}
.

Now, assume that
m\geq\sqrt{2}
. Coming back to (
3.2
), we deduce that if (
3.2
) is verified, then

\displaystyle n^{2}>-(2m^{2}+1)+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}=\frac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}}.

If

\displaystyle n^{2}\leq-(2m^{2}+1)+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}=\frac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}}<\frac{m^{2}}{2},

then we have

\displaystyle(m+n)^{2}(m-n)^{2}=(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}>\frac{m^{4}}{4}.

In this case, using the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\mu>\frac{(m^{2}-n^{2})^{2}}{1+n^{2}}\geq\frac{m^{4}}{4(1+n^{2})}\geq\frac{m^{4}}{2(1+2m^{2})},

which suffices to our purpose since this estimate is constant as the conformal class degenerates. Therefore, we can assume that

\displaystyle n^{2}>\frac{m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}{2m^{2}+1+\sqrt{5m^{4}+2m^{2}+1}},

(3.23)

and (
3.2
) can be solved as

\displaystyle Y<\sqrt[4]{n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt[4]{n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}}.

As
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we deduce that the right-hand side is bounded as (
3.23
) holds. Then, coming back to (
3.17
), we deduce that this condition holds if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\sqrt{2}}{Y}\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}+1+Y^{2}}{-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)}},

or

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}\left(-Y^{4}+n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)\right)\geq 25Y^{2}(Y^{2}+n^{2}+1),

or

\displaystyle n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)\geq\left(\frac{25}{2\pi^{2}}+1\right)Y^{4}+\frac{25}{2\pi^{2}}(1+n^{2})Y^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle Y^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{25}{4\pi^{2}}(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{\frac{525}{16\pi^{4}}(n^{2}+1)^{2}+\left(\frac{50}{\pi^{2}}+4\right)\left(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2)\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}{25(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}},

and finally

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\pi\sqrt{\frac{25(n^{2}+1)+\sqrt{525(n^{2}+1)^{2}+16\pi^{2}(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}}{2(50+\pi^{2})(n^{4}+2(2m^{2}+1)n^{2}-m^{2}(m^{2}-2))}}.

Step 5: Lower Estimate for All Eigenvalues.

In this step, we show that if the conformal class is large enough, then

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{m,n}>\frac{\left(2(m+n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(2(m-n)^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)}{4(n^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(3.24)

Therefore, let
\alpha=\dfrac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, and let

\displaystyle f(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2(m+t)^{2}+\alpha^{2})(2(m-t)^{2}+\alpha^{2})}{2(t^{2}+1)+\alpha^{2}}=\frac{4(m^{2}-t^{2})^{2}+4(m^{2}+t^{2})\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}}{2(t^{2}+1)+\alpha^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4t^{4}-4(2m^{2}-\alpha^{2})t^{2}+4m^{4}+4m^{2}\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}}{2t^{2}+2+\alpha^{2}}

As
f
is coercive, it admits a global minimum on
\mathbb{R}
. Since

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(4(m^{2}-t^{2})^{2}+4(m^{2}+t^{2})\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}\right)=-16t(m^{2}-t^{2})+8\alpha^{2}t=8t\left(2t^{2}-2m^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)

we get

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{4t}{(2t^{2}+2+\alpha^{2})^{2}}\left(2(2t^{2}-2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})(2t^{2}+2+\alpha^{2})-\left(4t^{4}-4(2m^{2}-\alpha^{2})t^{2}+4m^{4}+4m^{2}\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4t}{(2t^{2}+2+\alpha^{2})^{2}}\left(4t^{4}+4(2+\alpha^{2})t^{2}-4m^{2}(m^{2}+2)-4(2m^{2}-1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}\right).

Notice that for
\alpha=0
, we have

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)=\frac{2t}{(t^{2}+1)^{2}}\left(t^{2}(t^{2}+2)-m^{2}(m^{2}+2)\right),

which shows that
f^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
if and only if
t\geq m
or
-m\leq t\leq 0
, which implies that
\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f(t)=f(m)=0
. In the general case, we assume that

\displaystyle-4m^{2}(m^{2}+2)-4(2m^{2}-1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}\leq 0,

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\leq 2(2m^{2}-1)+2\sqrt{(2m^{2}-1)^{2}+m^{2}(m^{2}+2)},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{2(2m^{2}-1)+2\sqrt{(2m^{2}-1)^{2}+m^{2}(m^{2}+2)}}}.

(3.25)

The roots of

\displaystyle Q(X)=4X^{2}+4(2+\alpha^{2})X-4m^{2}(m^{2}+2)-4(2m^{2}-1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4}

are given by

\displaystyle r_{\pm}=-\frac{2+\alpha^{2}}{2}\pm\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(2+\alpha)^{2}+4m^{2}(m^{2}+2)+4(2m^{2}-1)\alpha^{2}-\alpha^{4}}.

If

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-\frac{\alpha^{2}+2}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\alpha^{2}+2)^{2}+4m^{2}(m^{2}+2)+4(2m^{2}-1)\alpha^{2}-\alpha^{4}}},

we deduce that
f^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
if and only if
-r\leq t\leq 0
or
t\geq r
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f(t)=f(r)=f(-r).

Then, notice that
r
can be written more simply as

\displaystyle r=\sqrt{-\frac{\alpha^{2}+2}{2}+\sqrt{(m^{2}+1)^{2}+2m^{2}\alpha^{2}}}.

We have

\displaystyle r\leq m

if and only if

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{(m^{2}+1)^{2}+2m^{2}\alpha^{2}}\leq 2(m^{2}+1)+\alpha^{2},

or

\displaystyle 4(m^{2}+1)^{2}+8m^{2}\alpha^{2}\leq 4(m^{2}+1)^{2}+4(m^{2}+1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4},

which is always true for
m=1
. Assuming now that
m>1
, we have
r\geq m
if and only if

\displaystyle 8m^{2}\alpha^{2}\geq 4(m^{2}+1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4},

or

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\leq 4(m^{2}-1),

which gives the condition

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{2\sqrt{m^{2}-1}}.

(3.26)

If this condition holds, as
f
is decreasing on
[m,r]
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f(t)=f(m)

provided that
r\leq m+1
and
f(m)<f(m+1)
. The condition
r\leq m+1
is equivalent to

\displaystyle 4(m^{2}+1)^{2}+8m^{2}\alpha^{2}\leq 4((m+1)^{2}+1)^{2}+4((m+1)^{2}+1)\alpha^{2}+\alpha^{4},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\alpha^{4}-4(m^{2}-2m-2)\alpha^{2}+4(2m+1)(2m^{2}+2m+3)\geq 0.

This condition is empty if
m\geq 1+\sqrt{3}
. Assuming that
m>1+\sqrt{3}
, we find the estimate (as we are interested in the limit when
\alpha\rightarrow 0
)

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\leq 2(m^{2}-2m-2)-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-2m-2)^{2}-(2m+1)(2m^{2}+2m+3)},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{2(m^{2}-2m-2)-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-2m-2)^{2}-(2m+1)(2m^{2}+2m+3)}},

(3.27)

provided that

\displaystyle(m^{2}-2m-2)^{2}-(2m+1)(2m^{2}+2m+3)>0,

or

\displaystyle m^{4}-8m^{3}-6m^{2}+1>0.

Otherwise, the condition on
\alpha
is empty. Let

\displaystyle g(t)=t^{4}-8t^{3}-6t^{2}+1.

We have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)=4t^{3}-24t^{2}-12t=4t(t^{2}-6t-3)=4t((t-3)^{2}-12)=4t(t-3-2\sqrt{3})(t-3+2\sqrt{3}).

Therefore, we deduce that
g^{\prime}(t)<0
for all
0<t<3+2\sqrt{3}
, and
g^{\prime}(t)>0
for all
t>3+2\sqrt{3}
. Therefore,
g
is strictly decreasing on
[0,2\sqrt{3}]
, and increasing on
\mathbb{R}\cap\left\{t:t\geq 2\sqrt{3}\right\}
. We have
2\sqrt{3}<4
, and
g(8)=-383<0
, while
g(9)=242>0
. Therefore, we only get a non-trivial condition provided that
m\geq 9
(or
m
larger than the largest root of
g
if we do not consider integer values, that can be expressed with cubic roots). Finally we deduce that for
0<a<b<\infty
such that all conditions (
3.25
), (
3.26
) (provided that
m>1
), and (
3.27
), we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f(t)=f(m)=\frac{\left(4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}},

(3.28)

which finally shows that (
3.24
) holds true.

Step 6: Minimal Eigenvalue.

Assume that the bounds (
3.25
), (
3.26
) (if
m>1
), and (
3.27
) from the previous step hold.
In this step, we show that if the conformal class is large enough,

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{m,n}=\mu_{m,m},

which is a refinement of the previous step. Thanks to the previous analysis, it is implied by the inequality

\displaystyle\frac{4\alpha^{2}(2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})}{m^{2}+1+2\alpha^{2}}\leq\inf_{n\neq m}\frac{(2(m+n)^{2}+\alpha^{2})(2(m-n)^{2}+\alpha)^{2}}{4(n^{2}+1)+2\alpha^{2}}.

Since the right-hand side is symmetric in
n
, it suffices to study the previous function
f
on
[m+1,\infty[
. Since
f
is increasing on this interval, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\neq m}\frac{(2(m+n)^{2}+\alpha^{2})(2(m-n)^{2}+\alpha)^{2}}{4(n^{2}+1)+2\alpha^{2}}=\frac{\left(2(2m+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left(2+\alpha^{2}\right)}{4(m^{2}+m+2)+2\alpha^{2}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\frac{4\alpha^{2}(2m^{2}+\alpha^{2})}{m^{2}+1+2\alpha^{2}}\leq\frac{\left(2(2m+1)^{2}+\alpha^{2}\right)\left(2+\alpha^{2}\right)}{4(m^{2}+m+2)+2\alpha^{2}}

if and only if (after some elementary computations)

\displaystyle 6\alpha^{6}+(15m^{2}+23)\alpha^{4}+4\left(6m^{4}+6m^{3}+5m^{2}-10m-3\right)\alpha^{2}\leq 4(2m+1)^{2}(m^{2}+1).

(3.29)

Assuming without loss of generality that
\alpha\leq 1
, we deduce that the inequality
(
3.29
) holds provided that

\displaystyle\left(32m^{4}+24m^{3}+35m^{2}-40m+11\right)\alpha^{2}\leq 4(2m+1)^{2}(m^{2}+1),

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{2(2m+1)}\sqrt{\frac{32m^{4}+24m^{3}+35m^{2}-40m+11}{m^{2}+1}},

(3.30)

which finally concludes the proof of the theorem if
m=1
or
m>1
and
n\neq 0
.

Assumption II:
n=0
and
m>1
.
In this case, we have

\displaystyle Q(Y)=Y^{4}-(2m^{2}-\mu)Y^{2}+m^{4}-\mu.

Its discriminant is given by

\displaystyle(2m^{2}-\mu)^{2}-4(m^{4}-\mu)=\mu(\mu-4(m^{2}-1)).

We need to distinguish three cases.

Case 1:
\mu>4(m^{2}-1)
.

If
\mu>4(m^{2}-1)
, then the previous proof is unchanged.

Case 2:
\mu=4(m^{2}-1)
.
Then

\displaystyle Q(Y)

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-(2m^{2}-4(m^{2}-1))Y^{2}+m^{4}-4m^{2}+4=Y^{4}-2(m^{2}-2)Y^{2}+(m^{2}-2)^{2}

\displaystyle=(Y^{2}-(m^{2}-2))^{2}.

Sub-case 1:
m>\sqrt{2}
.
Then
Q
admits two double roots
\pm\sqrt{m^{2}-2}
. Therefore, the solution
Y
takes the form

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}\,e^{(1+\sqrt{m^{2}-2})\,t}+\mu_{2}\,t\,e^{(1+\sqrt{m^{2}-2})\,t}+\mu_{3}\,e^{(1-\sqrt{m^{2}-2})\,t}+\mu_{4}\,t\,e^{(1-\sqrt{m^{2}-2})\,t}.

Letting
\lambda_{1}=1+\sqrt{m^{2}-2}
and
\lambda_{2}=1-\sqrt{m^{2}-2}
, the boundary conditions shows that the following matrix is not invertible:

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}a^{\lambda_{1}}&\log(a)a^{\lambda_{1}}&a^{\lambda_{2}}&\log(a)\,a^{\lambda_{2}}\\
b^{\lambda_{1}}&\log(b)b^{\lambda_{1}}&b^{\lambda_{2}}&\log(b)\,b^{\lambda_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}a^{\lambda_{1}}&(1+\lambda_{1}\log(a))a^{\lambda_{1}}&\lambda_{2}a^{\lambda_{1}}&(1+\lambda_{2}\log(a))a^{\lambda_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}b^{\lambda_{1}}&(1+\lambda_{1}\log(b))b^{\lambda_{1}}&\lambda_{2}b^{\lambda_{2}}&(1+\lambda_{2}\log(b))b^{\lambda_{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
X,Y\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
and
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
. We have

\displaystyle\begin{vmatrix}X^{\alpha}&\log(X)X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}&\log(X)X^{\beta}\\
Y^{\alpha}&\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}&\log(Y)Y^{\beta}\\
\alpha X^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}&\beta X^{\beta}&(1+\beta\log(X))X^{\beta}\\
\alpha Y^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}&\beta Y^{\beta}&(1+\beta\log(Y))Y^{\beta}\end{vmatrix}=-\log(X)X^{\beta}\begin{vmatrix}Y^{\alpha}&\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}\\
\alpha X^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}&\beta X^{\beta}\\
\alpha Y^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}&\beta Y^{\beta}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle+\log(Y)Y^{\beta}\begin{vmatrix}X^{\alpha}&\log(X)X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}\\
\alpha X^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}&\beta X^{\beta}\\
\alpha Y^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}&\beta Y^{\beta}\end{vmatrix}-(1+\beta\log(X))X^{\beta}\begin{vmatrix}X^{\alpha}&\log(X)X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}\\
Y^{\alpha}&\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}\\
\alpha Y^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}&\beta Y^{\beta}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle+(1+\beta\log(Y))Y^{\beta}\begin{vmatrix}X^{\alpha}&\log(X)X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}\\
Y^{\alpha}&\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}\\
\alpha X^{\alpha}&(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}&\beta X^{\beta}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\log(X)X^{\beta}\Big(-\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}\alpha\beta(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha})+(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}(\beta-\alpha)Y^{\alpha+\beta}

\displaystyle-(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}\left(\beta X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}-\alpha X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}\right)\Big)+\log(Y)Y^{\beta}\Big(-\log(X)X^{\alpha}\alpha\beta\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}\left(\beta X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-\alpha X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)-(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}(\beta-\alpha)X^{\alpha+\beta}\Big)

\displaystyle-\left(1+\beta\log(X)\right)X^{\beta}\Big(-\log(X)X^{\alpha}\left(\beta-\alpha\right)Y^{\alpha+\beta}+\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}\left(\beta X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-\alpha X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle-(1+\alpha\log(Y))Y^{\alpha}\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)\Big)+(1+\beta\log(Y))Y^{\beta}\Big(-\log(X)X^{\alpha}\left(\beta X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}-\alpha X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle+\log(Y)Y^{\alpha}\left(\beta-\alpha\right)X^{\alpha+\beta}-(1+\alpha\log(X))X^{\alpha}\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\log(X)\log(Y)\left(-2\alpha\beta\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)^{2}+2(\alpha X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-\beta X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha})(\beta X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-\alpha X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha})\right)

\displaystyle+(\beta-\alpha)^{2}\left(\log^{2}(X)+\log^{2}(Y)\right)X^{\alpha+\beta}Y^{\alpha+\beta}-\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)^{2}.

We can rewrite this expression (naming the underlying matrix
B
) as

\displaystyle\det(B)

\displaystyle=(\alpha-\beta)^{2}X^{\alpha+\beta}Y^{\alpha+\beta}\left(\log(X)^{2}-2\log(X)\log(Y)+\log^{2}(Y)\right)-(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\alpha-\beta)^{2}X^{\alpha+\beta}Y^{\alpha+\beta}\log^{2}\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)-\left(X^{\alpha}Y^{\beta}-X^{\beta}Y^{\alpha}\right)^{2}.

Therefore, we get if

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \alpha&=\lambda_{1}=1+\sqrt{m^{2}-2}=1+\mu\\
\beta&=\lambda_{2}=1-\sqrt{m^{2}-2}=1-\mu,\end{aligned}\right.

we get

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=4\mu^{2}a^{2}b^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-\left(a^{1+\mu}b^{1-\mu}-a^{1-\mu}b^{1+\mu}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{4}\left(4\mu^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\mu}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=a^{4}\left(2\mu\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}+\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\mu}\right)\right)\left(2\mu\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\mu}\right)\right).

Therefore, we have
\det(A)=0
if and only if

\displaystyle 2\mu\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\mu}\right)=0.

Therefore, we let
\alpha>0
and we introduce on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
the function

\displaystyle f(x)=2\alpha(1+x)^{\alpha+1}\log(1+x)-\left((1+x)^{2\alpha}-1\right).

We have

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=2\alpha(\alpha+1)(1+x)^{\alpha}\log(1+x)+2\alpha(1+x)^{\alpha}-2\alpha(1+x)^{2\alpha-1}

\displaystyle=2\alpha(1+x)^{\alpha}\left((\alpha+1)\log(1+x)+1-(1+x)^{\alpha-1}\right).

If
\alpha\leq 1
, we deduce that
f
is strictly increasing, and since
f(0)=0
, we deduce that
f(x)>0
for all
x>0
. If
\alpha>1
(or
m>\sqrt{3}
), let
\beta=\alpha-1>0
, and introduce the function

\displaystyle g(x)=(\beta+2)\log(1+x)+1-(1+x)^{\beta}.

Make the change of variable
1+x=e^{t}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle g(t)=(\beta+2)t+1-e^{\beta t}.

We have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(t)=\beta+2-\beta e^{\beta t}.

Therefore, if

\displaystyle\gamma=\frac{\log\left(\frac{\beta+2}{\beta}\right)}{\beta},

we deduce that
g
is strictly decreasing on
[0,\gamma]
and strictly increasing on
[\gamma,\infty[
. We have

\displaystyle g(\gamma)=(\beta+2)\gamma+1-\frac{(\beta+2)}{\beta}=\frac{\beta+2}{\beta}\left(\beta+\log\left(1+\frac{2}{\beta}\right)-1\right)>0.

Therefore, we deduce that there exists
\delta>\gamma
such that
f
is strictly increasing on
[0,\delta]
and strictly decreasing on
[\delta,\infty[
. Since
f(0)=0
,
f
admits a unique zero
r_{0}\in[e^{\gamma}-1,\infty[
. We have

\displaystyle f(t)

\displaystyle=2\alpha\,t\,e^{(\alpha+1)t}-(e^{2\alpha t}-1)=1+e^{(\alpha+1)t}\left(2\alpha t-e^{(\alpha-1)t}\right)

\displaystyle<1+e^{(\alpha+1)t}\left(2\alpha t-1-(\alpha-1)t-\frac{(\alpha-1)^{2}}{2}t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{2}e^{(\alpha+1)t}\left((\alpha-1)^{2}t^{2}-2(\alpha+1)t+2\right)<0

provided that
(\alpha-1)^{2}t^{2}-2(\alpha+1)t+2\geq 2
, or
t\geq\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{(\alpha-1)^{2}}
. Therefore, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\delta<e^{\frac{2(\alpha+1)}{(\alpha-1)^{2}}}

Since

\displaystyle 1+x=\frac{b}{a},

we conclude that there are no solutions provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\log\left(\frac{e^{2(\alpha+1)}}{(\alpha-1)^{2}}-1\right),

which is implied by the stronger condition

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{2\sqrt{m^{2}-2}}{(\sqrt{m^{2}-2}-1)^{2}}.

(3.31)

Notice that this condition is trivial provided that

\displaystyle\frac{2\sqrt{m^{2}-2}}{(\sqrt{m^{2}-2}-1)^{2}}\leq 1,

or

\displaystyle m^{4}-18m^{2}+33\geq 0,

(3.32)

which is satisfied on
[\sqrt{9+4\sqrt{3}},\infty[
.

To conclude, there are no solutions for
1<m\leq\sqrt{3}
and
m\geq\sqrt{9+4\sqrt{3}}
, and no solution provided that (
3.31
) if
\sqrt{3}<m<\sqrt{9+4\sqrt{3}}<4
.

We can now move to the next sub-case.

Sub-case 2:
m=\sqrt{2}
. Then
Q(Y)=Y^{4}
, and
P
admits
r=1
as quadruple root. Therefore, the solution
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{t}+\mu_{2}\,t\,e^{t}+\mu_{3}\,t^{2}\,e^{t}+\mu_{4}\,t^{3}\,e^{t}.

The boundary conditions show that the following matrix is not invertible:

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}a&\log(a)a&\log^{2}(a)a&\log^{3}(a)a\\
b&\log(b)b&\log^{2}(b)b&\log^{3}(b)b\\
a&(1+\log(a))a&(2\log(a)+\log^{2}(a))a&(3\log^{2}(a)+\log^{3}(a))a\\
b&(1+\log(b))b&(2\log(b)+\log^{2}(b))b&(3\log^{2}(b)+\log^{3}(b))b\end{pmatrix}.

A computation shows that

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=-(ab)^{2}\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right),

which is clearly never zero for all
0<a<b<\infty
.

Sub-case 3:
1<m<\sqrt{2}
. Then

\displaystyle Q(Y)=(Y^{2}+2-m^{2})^{2},

which shows that
Q
admits two double complex roots
\pm\,i\,\sqrt{2-m^{2}}
. If
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{2-m^{2}}
, we deduce that the roots of
P(X)=Q(X+1)
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=r_{2}&&=1+i\,\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}&=r_{4}&&=1-i\,\lambda_{1}\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore,
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}\,e^{(1+i\,\lambda_{1})t}+\mu_{2}\,t\,e^{(1+i\,\lambda_{1})}+\mu_{4}\,e^{(1-i\,\lambda_{1})t}+\mu_{4}\,t\,e^{(1-i\,\lambda_{1})t}.

The boundary conditions show that the following matrix is degenerate

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}a^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&\log(a)a^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&a^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}&\log(a)a^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
b^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&\log(b)b^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&b^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}&\log(b)b^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
(1+i\,\lambda_{1})a^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&(1+(1+i\,\lambda_{1})\log(a))a^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&(1-i\,\lambda_{1})a^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}&\left(1+(1-i\,\lambda_{1})\log(a)\right)a^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
(1+i\,\lambda_{1})b^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&(1+(1+i\,\lambda_{1})\log(b))b^{1+i\,\lambda_{1}}&(1-i\,\lambda_{1})b^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}&\left(1+(1-i\,\lambda_{1})\log(b)\right)b^{1-i\,\lambda_{1}}\end{pmatrix}.

We have

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=-\bigg(4\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(a\right)^{2}-8\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(a\right)\log\left(b\right)+4\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(b\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}+a^{4i\,\lambda_{1}}+b^{4i\,\lambda_{1}}\bigg)a^{-2i\,\lambda_{1}+2}b^{-2i\,\lambda_{1}+2}=0.

Therefore, we have the following identity

\displaystyle 4\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(a\right)^{2}-8\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(a\right)\log\left(b\right)+4\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{2}\log\left(b\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\,a^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\,\lambda_{1}}+a^{4i\,\lambda_{1}}+b^{4i\,\lambda_{1}}=0,

that is more simply rewritten as

\displaystyle 4\lambda_{1}^{2}a^{2i\lambda_{1}}b^{2i\lambda_{1}}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\left(b^{2i\lambda_{1}}-a^{2i\lambda_{1}}\right)^{2}=0,

or

\displaystyle 4\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2i\lambda_{1}}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2i\lambda_{1}}-1\right)^{2}=0.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &4\lambda_{1}^{2}\cos\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\left(\cos\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-1\right)^{2}-\sin^{2}\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0\\
&2\lambda_{1}^{2}\sin\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\cos\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\sin\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(3.33)

If
2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\notin\pi\mathbb{Z}
, we get

\displaystyle 2\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\cos\left(2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0,

which is impossible provided that

\displaystyle 2\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>1,

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\frac{1}{\sqrt{2(2-m^{2})}}.

Plugging this identity in the first equality of (
3.33
) shows that

\displaystyle 0=-8\lambda_{1}^{4}\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+\left(2\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-1\right)^{2}+4\lambda_{1}^{4}\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)-1

\displaystyle=-4\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right),

which is never
0
. Therefore, we deduce that either
2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=0
(mod
2\pi
) or
2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=\pi
(mod
2\pi
).
In the first alternative, the first identity of (
3.33
) becomes

\displaystyle 4\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=0,

which is never verified, while in the second alternative, it becomes

\displaystyle-4\lambda_{1}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)+4=0,

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2-m^{2}}}.

(3.34)

However, this identity implies that
2\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=2\neq\pi
, so we get a contradiction.

Finally, we can move to the third possibility.

Case 3:
\mu<4(m^{2}-1)
.

This is the expected case since
\mu
must go to
0
as the conformal class degenerates. Then,
Q(\sqrt{Y})
admits the roots

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(2m^{2}-\mu\pm\,i\,\sqrt{\mu(4(m^{2}-1)-\mu)}\right).

Let
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
be arbitrary, and let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle(\alpha+i\,\beta)^{2}=a+i\,b.

We get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}=a\\
2\alpha\beta=b\end{aligned}\right.

which implies that

\displaystyle\alpha^{4}-a\,\alpha^{2}-\frac{b^{2}}{4}=0,

and since
\alpha^{2}\geq 0
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left(a+\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\right),

and finally

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \alpha&=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\left(a+\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\right)}\\
\beta&=\mathrm{sgn}(b)\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\left(-a+\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\right)}\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, taking
a=\dfrac{1}{2}(2m^{2}-\mu)
and
b=\dfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{\mu(4(m^{2}-1)-\mu)}
, we get

\displaystyle a^{2}+b^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(4m^{4}-4m^{2}\mu+\mu^{2}+4(m^{2}-1)\mu-\mu^{2}\right)=m^{4}-\mu.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \alpha&=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}+2m^{2}-\mu\right)}\\
\beta&=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}-2m^{2}+\mu\right)},\end{aligned}\right.

(3.35)

while obviously

\displaystyle\left(\alpha-i\,\beta\right)^{2}=2m^{2}-\mu-i\,\sqrt{\mu(4(m^{2}-1)-\mu)}.

Finally, we deduce that the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}=1+\alpha+i\,\beta\\
r_{2}=1-\alpha-i\,\beta\\
r_{3}=1+\alpha-i\,\beta\\
r_{4}=1-\alpha+i\,\beta\end{aligned}\right.

If
\lambda_{1}=\alpha+i\,\beta
and
\lambda_{2}=\alpha-i\,\beta
, the boundary conditions shows that the following matrix is not invertible

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}a^{1+\lambda_{1}}&a^{1-\lambda_{1}}&a^{1+\lambda_{2}}&a^{1-\lambda_{2}}\\
b^{1+\lambda_{1}}&b^{1-\lambda_{1}}&b^{1+\lambda_{2}}&b^{1-\lambda_{2}}\\
(1+\lambda_{1})a^{1+\lambda_{1}}&(1-\lambda_{1})a^{1-\lambda_{1}}&(1+\lambda_{2})a^{1+\lambda_{2}}&(1-\lambda_{2})a^{1-\lambda_{2}}\\
(1+\lambda_{1})b^{1+\lambda_{1}}&(1-\lambda_{1})b^{1-\lambda_{1}}&(1+\lambda_{2})b^{1+\lambda_{2}}&(1-\lambda_{2})b^{1-\lambda_{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

We have

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle={\left(a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times{\left(a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times a^{-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+2}b^{-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+2}.

Therefore, we either have

\displaystyle a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}=0

(3.36)

or

\displaystyle a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}=0.

(3.37)

Dividing these equation by
a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}
shows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}}\left(a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}+1-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)+\lambda_{2}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}-1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}\right)-\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}-1\right),

(3.38)

while the second identity becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}}\left(a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{1}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{2}}b^{\lambda_{1}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}}b^{\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{1}+a^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}-b^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\lambda_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}-1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)+\lambda_{2}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}+1-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}\right)+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}-1\right).

(3.39)

Recalling that
\lambda_{1}=\alpha+i\,\beta
and
\lambda_{2}=\alpha-i\,\beta
, we deduce that

\displaystyle(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{2}}\right)-\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}-1\right)

\displaystyle=4\,i\,\beta\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\alpha}\sin\left(\beta\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\,i\,\beta\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\alpha}-1\right),

and since
\beta\neq 0
, we get that (
3.36
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\alpha}\sin\left(\beta\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\alpha}-1,

(3.40)

while the second equation (
3.37
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\alpha}\sin\left(\beta\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=-\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\alpha}-1\right),

(3.41)

Recall that by (
3.35
), we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \alpha&=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}+2m^{2}-\mu\right)}\\
\beta&=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}-2m^{2}+\mu\right)},\end{aligned}\right.

(3.42)

Let also
R=\log\left(\dfrac{b}{a}\right)
.

Second Equality.
We look for the first positive zero of
f:[0,4(m^{2}-1)]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle f(t)=2\,e^{\frac{R}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}+2m^{2}-t\right)}}\sin\left(\frac{R}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}-2m^{2}+t\right)}\right)+\left(e^{R\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}+2m^{2}-t\right)}}-1\right).

We have
f(t)>0
on
[0,\mu]
provided that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}-2m^{2}+\mu\right)}\leq\frac{2\pi}{R},

or

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{m^{4}-\mu}\leq\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+2m^{2}-\mu,

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle 4(m^{4}-\mu)\leq 4m^{4}+\frac{16m^{2}\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\frac{16\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}-2\left(2m^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)\mu+\mu^{2},

or

\displaystyle\mu^{2}-4\left((m^{2}-1)+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)\mu+\frac{16m^{2}\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\frac{16\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}\leq 0.

The discriminant of this quadratic polynomial in
\mu
is given by

\displaystyle 16(m^{2}-1)^{2}+\frac{64(m^{2}-1)\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\frac{64\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}-\frac{64m^{2}\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}-\frac{64\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}=16\left((m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right).

Assuming that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{2\pi}{m^{2}-1},

we deduce that

\displaystyle 2\left((m^{2}-1)+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}<\mu<2\left((m^{2}-1)+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}.

In particular, we get in this alternative

\displaystyle\mu_{m,0}

\displaystyle>2\left((m^{2}-1)+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)^{2}-2\left((m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}}=\frac{\dfrac{8m^{2}\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\dfrac{8\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}}

or

\displaystyle\mu_{m,n}

\displaystyle>\frac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}.

(3.43)

First Equality.
Then, we look for the first positive zero of the function
f:[0,4(m^{2}-1)]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that for all
t\in[0,4(m^{2}-1)]
,

\displaystyle f(t)=2\,e^{\frac{R}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}+2m^{2}-t\right)}}\sin\left(\frac{R}{2}\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}-2m^{2}+t\right)}\right)-\left(e^{R\sqrt{\left(2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}+2m^{2}-t\right)}}-1\right).

It it unclear how to directly estimate the first zero of this function, so we will have to differentiate it. Make the change of variable

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{4}-t}=2s,

or

\displaystyle t=m^{4}-4s^{2}.

We get

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}+2m^{2}-t=4s+2m^{2}-(m^{4}-4s^{2})=4s(s+1)-(m^{2}-1)^{2}+1

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{m^{4}-t}-2m^{2}+t=4s-2m^{2}+m^{4}-4s^{2}=4s(1-s)+(m^{2}-1)^{2}-1,

and finally, if
4p=(m^{2}-1)^{2}-1
, we get

\displaystyle g(s)=f(m^{4}-4s^{2})=2e^{R\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}\sin\left(R\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}\right)-\left(e^{2R\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}-1\right).

Notice that the domain of definition of
s
is

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{m^{4}-4(m^{2}-1)},\frac{1}{2}m^{2}\right]=\left[\frac{1}{2}|m^{2}-2|,\frac{1}{2}m^{2}\right].

Now, we have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(s)=\frac{R(2s+1)}{\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}e^{R\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}\sin\left(R\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{R(-2s+1)}{\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}}e^{R\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}\cos\left(R\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}\right)-\frac{2R(2s+1)}{\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}e^{2R\sqrt{s(s+1)-p}}.

Notice that

\displaystyle f(0)=-\left(e^{R\sqrt{2\sqrt{m^{4}}+2m^{2}}}-1\right)=-\left(e^{2m^{2}R}-1\right)<0,

and since
t=m^{4}-4s^{2}
, we need to give an
upper
estimate of the first zero
|m^{2}-2|/2<s_{1}<m^{2}/2
of
g
. Notice that provided that
0\leq R\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}\leq\pi/2
, and
1/2\leq s\leq m^{2}/2
, we have
g^{\prime}(s)<0
. Recalling that
p=\dfrac{1}{4}((m^{2}-1)^{2}-1)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle 0\leq R\sqrt{p-s(s-1)}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}

if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}((m^{2}-1)^{2}-1)-s(s-1)\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{4R^{2}},

or

\displaystyle s^{2}-s-\frac{1}{4}((m^{2}-1)^{2}-1)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{4R^{2}}\geq 0.

The discriminant of this polynomial function is given by

\displaystyle(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}.

Assuming that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{m^{2}-1},

we deduce that

\displaystyle s\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}.

In other words the first zero
s_{1}<m^{2}/2
is such that

\displaystyle s_{1}<\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}.

Therefore, the first positive zero
t_{1}>0
of
f
is such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{m^{4}-t_{1}}<1+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}},

or

\displaystyle m^{4}-t_{1}<1+(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}=m^{4}-2m^{2}+1-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}},

and finally

\displaystyle t_{1}

\displaystyle>2m^{2}-1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}=\frac{4m^{4}-4m^{2}+1+2(2m^{2}-1)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}-4(m^{2}-1)^{2}+\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}{2m^{2}-1+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{4m^{2}-3+2(2m^{2}+1)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{R^{4}}}{2m^{2}-1+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{R^{2}}}}=\dfrac{4m^{2}-3+2(2m^{2}+1)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2m^{2}-1+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}.

Therefore, the eigenvalue
\mu_{m,0}
satisfies in this alternative the inequality

\displaystyle\mu_{m,0}>\dfrac{4m^{2}-3+2(2m^{2}+1)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2m^{2}-1+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}},

(3.44)

provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi}{m^{2}-1}.

Then, let us estimate a minimum on the conformal class such that

\displaystyle\dfrac{4m^{2}-3+2(2m^{2}+1)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2m^{2}-1+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}\geq\frac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}.

Letting
X=\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, this inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2m^{2}-1+X^{2}-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-X^{2}}\geq 2\left((m^{2}-1)+2X^{2}\right)-2\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-4X^{2}},

or

\displaystyle 4m-3-3X^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\left(\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-X^{2}}-\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-4X^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{6X^{2}}{\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-X^{2}}+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-4X^{2}}}.

(3.45)

Assuming that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{4\pi}{m^{2}-1},

(3.46)

we deduce that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)-X^{2}}+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-4X^{2}}\geq\dfrac{\sqrt{3}}{4}\left(\sqrt{5}+2\right)(m^{2}-1),

and in particular (
3.2
) is implied by the inequality

\displaystyle 4m-3\geq\left(3+\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}\left(\sqrt{5}+2\right)\right)X^{2},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{2\pi}{3(2+\sqrt{3}(\sqrt{5}+2))(4m-3)}.

(3.47)

Then, we can move to the general lower bound on all eigenvalues. Using (
3.28
) (and assuming that (
3.26
), (
3.27
), and (
3.30
) hold), we look for a condition on the conformal class so that

\displaystyle\dfrac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}\geq\dfrac{\left(4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

We have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}\geq\dfrac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{2(m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\dfrac{b}{a}\right)}},

showing that the inequality is true provided that

\displaystyle 2(m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\leq 2(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu_{m,n}=\mu_{m,m}

provided that

\displaystyle\dfrac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{\left((m^{2}-1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)+\sqrt{(m^{2}-1)^{2}-\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}}\geq\frac{\left(8m^{2}+\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{m^{2}+1+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}},

which is only asymptotically true for
m\leq\sqrt{3}<2
. Since
m\geq 2
in this last step, we cannot conclude which eigenvalue is the smallest between
\mu_{m,0}
and
\mu_{m,m}
. This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

3.3
Second Weighted Estimate

We deduce the following near-optimal weighted inequality.

Theorem 3.2
.

For all
m\geq 1
, there exists
R_{m}<\infty
with the following property. Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. Assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq R_{m}.

Then, for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{\left(4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

4
Second Class of Weighted Poincaré Estimates

4.1
Inequalities Associated to the Bilaplacian

We will now generalise the last two inequalities of
[
8
, Lemma IV.
1
]
. If
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
, recall that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, if

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}=\Delta+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}},

then

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+(m^{2}-1)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{2}dx+4(m^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u-\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{2}dx.

Before stating the theorem, let us prove an elementary lemma that will prove crucial in all cases
m\geq 1
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta<\infty
and
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}dx=-\frac{1}{2\beta-1}\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u}{|x|^{2(1-\beta)}}\frac{x}{|x|^{2(1-\beta)}}\cdot\nabla u\,dx.

(4.1)

Furthermore, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}dx\leq\frac{1}{(2\beta-1)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}|x|^{4\beta}dx.

(4.2)

Proof.

First, notice that since
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
, we have by the Sobolev embedding
u\in C^{0}(B(0,1))
, which implies that there exists a universal constant
C<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{2,2}(B(0,1))}\leq C\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

where we used (
2.13
) (that holds in the limiting case
a=0
and
b=1
). Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}dx\leq C\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B(0,1))}^{2}\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{dx}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}=\frac{\pi C}{2\beta-1}\left\|\Delta u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}<\infty.

Since
\beta>\dfrac{1}{2}
, we have
|x|^{4\beta-2}\Delta\log|x|=2\pi|x|^{4\beta-2}\delta_{0}=0
, which shows by an integration by parts that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\,|x|^{4\beta}dx=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{4\beta-2}\nabla\log|x|\cdot\nabla(u^{2})\,dx=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{B(0,1)}\nabla(|x|^{4\beta-2})\cdot\nabla\log|x|\,u^{2}dx=-(2\beta-1)\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4(1-\beta)}}dx.

(4.3)

The inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
∎

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
and
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. Then, for all
1/2<\beta<\infty
, there exists a universal constant
C_{\beta}^{\prime}<\infty
independent of
0<a<b<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(4.4)

For all
\sqrt{2}-1<\beta<\infty
, there exists a universal constant
C_{\beta}^{\prime}<\infty
independent of
0<a<b<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}\right)\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq C_{\beta}^{\prime}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(4.5)

Proof.

Step 1: First integral, first part.

Since
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we can extend it—without changing notations—by
0
as a
W^{2,2}
function for which all
L^{2}
norms (of
u
and its derivatives) are preserved.
Making the change of variable
y=\dfrac{x}{b}
, we get if
\overline{u}(y)=u(b\,y)
(
y\in B(0,1)
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=\frac{1}{b^{2}}\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{b^{-1}a}(0)}|y|^{4\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy=\frac{1}{b^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy.

(4.6)

Thanks
[
19
]
, the Sobolev constant in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
for
W^{1,p}
functions is given by

\displaystyle C(2,p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{1}{2^{p}}\left(\frac{p-1}{2-p}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left(\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{2}{p}\right)\Gamma\left(3-\frac{2}{p}\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{1}{2^{p}}\left(\frac{p-1}{2-p}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{1}{(2-p)^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}.

Therefore, for all
q>1
such that
4(1-\beta)p^{\prime}<2
, or

\displaystyle 2(1-\beta)<\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1-\frac{1}{p}\Longleftrightarrow p>\frac{1}{2\beta-1},

which implies in particular that we must choose
\beta>1/2
. Then, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{dy}{|y|^{4(1-\beta)p^{\prime}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\overline{u}|^{2p}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(2,\frac{2p}{p+1}\right)^{2}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{dy}{|y|^{4(1-\beta)p^{\prime}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\overline{u}|^{\frac{2p}{p+1}}dy\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(2,\frac{2p}{p+1}\right)^{2}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{dy}{|y|^{4(1-\beta)p^{\prime}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\pi^{\frac{1}{p+1}}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}dy

(4.7)

Then, by the standard Poincaré inequality, the parallelogram identity, and (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}dy=4\int_{B(0,1)}|\partial_{z}\overline{u}|^{2}dy\leq\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla\partial_{z}u|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(\left|(\partial_{z}+\partial_{\overline{z}})\partial_{z}\overline{u}\right|^{2}+|i(\partial_{z}-\partial_{\overline{z}})\partial_{z}\overline{u}|^{2}\right)dy=\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(2|\partial_{z}^{2}\overline{u}|^{2}+2|\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}\overline{u}|^{2}\right)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla^{2}\overline{u}|^{2}dy=\frac{16}{\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(\Delta\overline{u}\right)^{2}dy.

(4.8)

Therefore, we deduce by (
4.1
) and (
4.1
) that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy\leq\frac{16}{\pi^{2-\frac{1}{p+1}}}C\left(2,\frac{2p}{p+1}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{1-2(1-\beta)p^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta\overline{u})^{2}dy.

(4.9)

Since

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta\overline{u})^{2}dy=b^{4}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(\Delta u(b\,y)\right)dy=b^{2}\int_{B(0,b)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx=b^{2}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx,

(4.10)

we finally deduce by (
4.6
), (
4.9
), and (
4.10
) that for all
\frac{1}{2}<\beta<1
and
p>\frac{1}{2\beta-1}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq\frac{16}{\pi^{2-\frac{1}{p+1}}}C\left(2,\frac{2p}{p+1}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\pi}{1-2(1-\beta)p^{\prime}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(4.11)

Step 2: First integral, inversion part.

Making an inversion as in
[
8
, Lemma IV.
1
]
, let us show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq\frac{1}{((2\beta+1)^{2}-2)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(4.12)

Now, making the change of variable

\displaystyle a\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}=y

we get

\displaystyle\frac{dx}{|x|^{4}}=\frac{dy}{a^{2}}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=\frac{1}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{b^{-1}a}(0)}|y|^{4\beta}|u(a\,\iota(y))|^{2}dy=\frac{1}{a^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}|\overline{u}(y)|^{2}dy,

where
\overline{u}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the extension by
0
of
u(a\,\iota(\,\cdot\,))
, where
\iota(x)=\dfrac{x}{|x|^{2}}
is the inversion. Now, applying (
4.2
) to the exponent
\beta+1>1/2
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}|\overline{u}(y)|^{2}dy\leq\frac{1}{(2\beta+1)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\left(y\cdot\nabla\overline{u}\right)^{2}|y|^{4\beta}dy\leq\frac{1}{(2\beta+1)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta+2}|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}dy.

(4.13)

Integrating by parts as above, we get by (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta+2}|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta+2}\overline{u}\,\Delta\overline{u}\,dy-(4\beta+2)\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{\overline{u}}{|y|^{2}}\frac{y}{|y|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\overline{u}\,|y|^{4\beta+4}dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta+2}\overline{u}\,\Delta\overline{u}\,dy+2\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}|\overline{u}|^{2}dy.

(4.14)

Therefore, (
4.13
) and (
4.1
) show that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}|\overline{u}|^{2}dy\leq-\frac{1}{(2\beta+1)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta+2}\overline{u}\,\Delta\overline{u}\,dy+\frac{2}{(2\beta+1)^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{4\beta}|\overline{u}|^{2}dy.

(4.15)

Since
\beta>\dfrac{}{}
, we have
\dfrac{2}{(2\beta+1)^{2}}\leq\dfrac{1}{2}<1
