We now move to the estimate of logarithm components. For this, we will need to bound
b_{0}
. We have by (
4.1
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\gamma+1}a_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{b^{2}}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}b_{0}^{2}a^{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4(\gamma+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}a_{0}b_{0}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\gamma}dx.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\gamma+1}a_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{a^{2}}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma+1)}\right)+\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}b_{0}^{2}a^{2}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}a_{0}b_{0}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}\right)\geq b_{0}^{2}a^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma-1)}\right)-\frac{2\gamma+1}{4\gamma^{2}}\frac{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}\right)^{2}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma+1)}}\right).

As previously, observer that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma-1)}\right)-\frac{2\gamma+1}{4\gamma^{2}}\frac{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}\right)^{2}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\gamma+1)}}\underset{\frac{b}{a}\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}-\frac{2\gamma+1}{4\gamma^{2}}=\frac{1}{4\gamma^{2}(2\gamma-1)}>0.

Now, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\gamma-1}\left(1-x^{2(2\gamma-1)}\right)-\frac{2\gamma+1}{4\gamma^{2}}\frac{\left(1-x^{4\gamma}\right)^{2}}{1-x^{2(2\gamma+1)}}\geq\frac{1}{8\gamma^{2}(2\gamma-1)}>0

if and only if

\displaystyle 8\gamma^{2}\left(1-x^{2(2\gamma-1)}-x^{2(2\gamma+1)}+x^{8\gamma}\right)-2(4\gamma^{2}-1)\left(1-2x^{4\gamma}+x^{8\gamma}\right)\geq 1-x^{2(2\gamma+1)}.

Now, notice that the condition (
4.52
) implies that

\displaystyle 8\gamma^{2}\left(1-x^{2(2\gamma-1)}-x^{2(2\gamma+1)}+x^{8\gamma}\right)-2(4\gamma^{2}-1)\left(1-2x^{4\gamma}+x^{8\gamma}\right)\geq 1.

In particular, if (
4.52
) holds, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8\gamma^{2}(2\gamma-1)}b_{0}^{2}a^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\gamma}dx.

(4.74)

However, this estimate does not suffice since we need to bound
b_{0}^{2}b^{2}
. Therefore, we will use the estimate from
Step 1
.
Now, recall that for all
0<a<e^{2}\,b<\infty
, we have by (
4.48
)

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{2}}{a^{2}}\leq\frac{8}{\pi}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}dx.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi\alpha^{2}}{\gamma+1}\left(\frac{1}{a^{2}}-\frac{1}{b^{2}}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)\leq\frac{32}{\gamma+1}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\gamma+1)}\right)\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}dx.

(4.75)

Using the previous estimates (
4.60
), (
4.50
), and (
4.53
), if
0<b<e^{-8}
and

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log(13)+\frac{3}{4}\log(2)-1,

and (
4.54
) holds and
0<a<e^{2}\,b<e^{-8}
, then

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &2\pi\beta^{2}\log^{2}(b)\leq 64\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}dx+\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\psi)^{2}dx\\
&\pi\beta^{2}b^{2}\leq\frac{8}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}\\
&\pi b_{0}^{2}b^{2}\leq 8\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}dx\end{aligned}\right.

(4.76)

Therefore, we estimate

\displaystyle\frac{16\pi\beta^{2}}{1-\gamma}\left(b^{2}\left(\log^{2}(b)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log(b)+\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}-a^{2}\left(\log^{2}(a)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log(a)+\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{24\pi\beta^{2}}{1-\gamma}b^{2}\log^{2}(b)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\frac{16\pi\beta^{2}}{(1-\gamma)}b^{2}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\leq\frac{768\,\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{12}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\psi)^{2}dx+\frac{128}{1-\gamma}\frac{\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{768\,\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}dx+\frac{32}{1-\gamma}\left(3+\frac{2}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx.

(4.77)

Finally, we get by (
4.76
)

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi(\beta+2b_{0})^{2}}{1-\gamma}\left(b^{2}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}-a^{2}\right)\leq\frac{8\pi\beta^{2}}{1-\gamma}b^{2}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}\right)+\frac{32b_{0}^{2}}{1-\gamma}b^{2}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}dx+\frac{256\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)}{1-\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}dx.

(4.78)

Therefore, for all
\dfrac{1}{2}<\gamma,\delta<1
if (
4.64
) holds, we deduce by (
4.1
) and the estimates (
4.1
), (
4.1
), (
4.72
), (
4.73
),(
4.75
), (
4.1
), and (
4.1
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\psi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}dx\leq\frac{16}{\gamma+1}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\gamma+1)}\right)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{768\,\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}dx+\frac{32}{1-\gamma}\left(3+\frac{2}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\right)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{256\,\delta^{2}(2\delta+1)}{1-\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\gamma}dx+\frac{16}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{6}{\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx+\frac{8\delta(2\delta+1)^{2}}{\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\delta}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\delta}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{10}{1-\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\left(2+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\frac{5-5\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}+2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4}}{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{8\,\delta}{\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\delta}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\delta}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{256\,\delta^{2}}{1-\gamma}\left(3\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\right)\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{8\,\delta((2\delta+1)^{2}+1)}{\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4\delta}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\delta}dx

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{16}{\gamma+1}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\gamma+1)}\right)+\frac{6}{\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}+\frac{16}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}\left(6+\frac{5-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(1-\gamma)}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{10}{1-\gamma}\frac{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}}{1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}}\left(2+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\frac{5-5\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}+2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{4}}{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx,

(4.79)

where we used the identity

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx=2\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}\psi|^{2}dx+\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\psi)^{2}dx.

Finally, using (
4.65
) and (
4.1
), we deduce that for all
\dfrac{1}{2}<\gamma,\delta<1
, if

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\max\left\{2,\frac{1}{2\delta-1}\log\left(4\delta\right),\frac{1}{4\delta}\log\left(\frac{2}{2-\sqrt{3}}\right),\frac{1}{4(1-\delta)}\log\left(1+\frac{8\delta(1-\delta)}{(2\delta-1)^{2}}\right),\frac{1}{4\delta}\log(8\delta(\delta+1))\right\},

(4.80)

then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\psi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{\gamma,\delta}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\delta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\delta}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx\right)

Notice that in fact, the proof of this step works for all
0<\gamma<1
(provided that integrals involving the
L^{2}
norm of
\psi
are taken with respect to the
\delta
weight). Furthermore, the hypothesis
0<a<b\leq e^{-8}
is not restrictive by an immediate scaling argument. Therefore, the proof is complete.
∎

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
and
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. For all
\dfrac{1}{2}<\beta<1
and
\sqrt{2}-1<\gamma<1
there exists a universal constant
C_{\beta,\gamma}<\infty
such that
for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)
, provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\max\left\{2,\frac{1}{2\beta-1}\log\left(4\beta\right),\frac{1}{4\beta}\log\left(\frac{2}{2-\sqrt{3}}\right),\frac{1}{4(1-\beta)}\log\left(1+\frac{8\beta(1-\beta)}{(2\beta-1)^{2}}\right),\frac{1}{4\beta}\log(8\beta(\beta+1))\right\},

(4.81)

we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta,\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx\right).

(4.82)

Proof.

On
\Omega
, make the decomposition
u=\varphi+\psi
, where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}\varphi&=\Delta^{2}u\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\varphi&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\partial\Omega\\
\partial_{\nu}\varphi&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\partial\Omega,\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}\psi&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
\psi&=u\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega\\
\partial_{\nu}\psi&=\partial_{\nu}u\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

Step 2:
Estimate of

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

Thanks to the estimate of Theorem
4.2
, we deduce that for all
\gamma>\sqrt{2}-1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{\gamma}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\varphi)^{2}dx.

(4.83)

Thanks to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\varphi)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}\varphi\,\Delta^{2}\varphi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\varphi\,\Delta^{2}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\Delta\varphi\,\Delta u\,dx\leq\left(\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\varphi)^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.84)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\varphi)^{2}dx\leq\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx,

(4.85)

and (
4.83
) and (
4.85
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{\gamma}^{\prime}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

Furthermore, the inequality from Theorem
shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\varphi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\varphi)^{2}dx\leq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

On the other hand, using Theorem
4.3
for the biharmonic function
\psi
, we deduce by (
4.85
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\psi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\psi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\psi|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\varphi^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx

\displaystyle+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\varphi|^{2}dx+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\left(1+C_{\beta}\right)\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx

where we used the fact that for all
\chi\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}\chi|^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}(\Delta\chi)^{2}dx.

Therefore, we finally get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\varphi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle+2\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla\psi|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 4\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+2\left(C_{\gamma}^{\prime}+2\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\left(1+C_{\beta}\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx+4\,C_{\beta,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx

which concludes the proof of the corollary.
∎

4.2
General Case

For
m>1
, the proof is a bit more involved, but the inequality is more precise.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
m>1
, and let

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}=\Delta+2(m-1)\cdot\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
and
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. Then, for all
0<\alpha<\min\left\{\dfrac{4\,m}{3},2\right\}
, there exists a universal constant
C_{m,\alpha}<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx&&\leq C_{m,\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx\\
&\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx&&\leq C_{m,\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx\end{aligned}\right.

(4.86)

Remark 4.7
.

In particular, for all
\alpha>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}+\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx\leq C_{\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}=\Delta^{2}+2(m^{2}-1)\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\Delta-4(m^{2}-1)\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)+\frac{(m^{2}-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}.

(4.87)

We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}u\,\Delta\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx-2(m-1)\int_{\Omega}u\,\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+\int_{\Omega}\Delta\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+2\int_{\Omega}u\,\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx

\displaystyle-2(m-1)\int_{\Omega}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\,\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+2\int_{\Omega}u\,\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx

\displaystyle-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx,

(4.88)

where we used the identities

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \nabla|x|^{2\alpha}=2\alpha\,x|x|^{2\alpha-2}\\
\Delta|x|^{2\alpha}=4\alpha^{2}|x|^{2\alpha-2}.\end{aligned}\right.

By (
4.87
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(u\,\Delta^{2}u\,+2(m^{2}-1)\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}u\,\Delta u-4(m^{2}-1)u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(m^{2}-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}u^{2}\right)dx.

(4.89)

Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\Delta^{2}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u\right)^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}u\,\Delta u\Delta\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)dx+2\int_{\Omega}\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\right)\cdot\nabla u\,\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}(\Delta u)^{2}dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\,\Delta u\,dx.

(4.90)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left((\Delta u)^{2}+2(m^{2}-1)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u+\frac{(m^{2}-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}u^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle-4(m^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{(m^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle-4(m^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx.

(4.91)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{(m^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx

\displaystyle+\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle-4(m^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx

(4.92)

Then, integrating by parts, we get

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx=-8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx-4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx

\displaystyle=-8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx-16(m-1)\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\,dx-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle-4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=-8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx-4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

(4.93)

while

\displaystyle\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle=\left(8\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx+(m-1)^{2}(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.94)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx=-\cancel{8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx}-\cancel{4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx}

\displaystyle-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+\left(\cancel{8\alpha^{2}}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+\cancel{4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx}+(m-1)^{2}(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+\left(2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle-(m-1)^{2}\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.95)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha-2}u\right)\cdot\nabla u\,dx=-2(\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx.

(4.96)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx=-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+\left(2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)-8(m-1)\alpha(1-\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx-(m-1)^{2}\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.97)

Now, recall the following computation

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(u^{2}\right)\,dx=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}\dive\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\,u^{2}\right)dx

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx,

(4.98)

where we used that

\displaystyle\dive\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)=\Delta\log|x|=0\qquad\text{in}\;\,\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\left\{0\right\}).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx=-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\Big(2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)-8(m-1)\alpha(1-\alpha)\Big)(1-\alpha)

\displaystyle-(m-1)^{2}(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha)\bigg\}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.99)

Now, thanks to (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}u.

Therefore, using polar coordinates, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx=\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-1}u(r,\theta)\partial_{r}^{2}u(r,\theta)dr\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-1}(\partial_{r}u(r,\theta))^{2}dr\right)d\theta-(2\alpha-1)\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-2}u(r,\theta)\partial_{r}u(r,\theta)dr\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-1}(\partial_{r}u(r,\theta))^{2}dr\right)d\theta-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2}\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-2}\partial_{r}(u(r,\theta))^{2}dr\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-1}(\partial_{r}u(r,\theta))^{2}dr\right)d\theta+(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\int_{S^{1}}\left(\int_{a}^{b}r^{2\alpha-3}(u(r,\theta))^{2}dr\right)d\theta

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}|\partial_{r}u|^{2}dx+(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx,

(4.100)

Finally, by (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{(m^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx-8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\Big(2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)\alpha)-\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)-8(m-1)\alpha(1-\alpha)\Big)(1-\alpha)

\displaystyle-(m-1)^{2}(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha)\bigg\}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+4(m^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-4(m^{2}-1)(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{(m^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)(m+1-2\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx-4(m-1)(2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha+(m+1))\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.101)

If
2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha+(m+1)\leq 0
, we are done, so assume that
2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha+(m+1)>0
. First, notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.102)

Then, the elementary identity (
4.2
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality imply that for all
\alpha<1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.103)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
), (
4.102
), and (
4.103
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx\geq\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\left(m+1-2\alpha+\alpha-\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\left(2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha+(m+1)\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle-\frac{4(m-1)\alpha}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\left(\alpha^{2}-(m+1)\alpha+m^{2}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx.

Since

\displaystyle(m+1)^{2}-4m^{2}=-3m^{2}+2m+1<0\quad\text{for all}\;\,m>1,

we deduce that for all
m>1
and
\alpha>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}dx\leq\frac{(1+\alpha)^{2}}{4(m-1)\alpha(\alpha^{2}+(m+1)\alpha+m^{2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1+\alpha)^{2}}{4(m-1)\alpha(\alpha^{2}+(m+1)\alpha+m^{2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx.

Furthermore, the inequality (
4.103
) shows that for all
m>1
and for all
\alpha>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\alpha}{4(m-1)\alpha(\alpha^{2}+(m+1)\alpha+m^{2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\alpha}{4(m-1)\alpha(\alpha^{2}+(m+1)\alpha+m^{2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx.

For the second inequality with the weight
\left(\dfrac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}
, we will take advantage of the quasi conformal invariance of our problem and of the almost self-adjointness properties of
\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}
.

Now, making the change of variable

\displaystyle\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}=y

we get

\displaystyle\frac{dx}{|x|^{4}}=dy.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{-2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|y|^{2\alpha}|u(i(y))|^{2}dy=\int_{\Omega^{\prime})}|y|^{2\alpha}|\overline{u}(y)|^{2}dy,

(4.104)

where
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{a^{-1}}\setminus\overline{B}_{b^{-1}}(0)
, and
where
\overline{u}=u\circ\iota:\Omega^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Then, thanks to (
4.1
), if
\overline{u}=u(\iota(x))
, then

\displaystyle\Delta\overline{u}=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\Delta u(\iota(x)).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle x\cdot\nabla\overline{u}

\displaystyle=x_{1}\left(\frac{x_{2}^{2}-x_{1}^{2}}{(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{x_{1}}u-\frac{2x_{1}x_{2}}{(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{x_{2}}u\right)+x_{2}\left(-\frac{2x_{1}x_{2}}{(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})}\partial_{x_{1}}u\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u(\iota(x)).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\overline{u}-2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\overline{u}+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\overline{u}=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\Delta u\circ\iota+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{4}}\cdot\nabla u\circ\iota+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\circ\iota,

or more compactly

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\overline{u}=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)\circ\iota

(4.105)

which implies that for all
b>1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|y|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta\overline{u}-2(m-1)\frac{y}{|y|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\overline{u}+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|y|^{2}}\overline{u}\right)^{2}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2\alpha}}\left(|x|^{4}\Delta u+2(m-1)|x|^{2}\,x\cdot\nabla u+(m-1)^{2}|x|^{2}u\right)^{2}\frac{dx}{|x|^{4}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}{|x|^{4-2\alpha}}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.106)

Now, recall that by (
2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m}=\Delta^{2}+2(m^{2}-1)\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\Delta-4(m^{2}-1)\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}\left(\,\cdot\,\right)\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)+\frac{(m^{2}-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}},

while (
2.5
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}=\widetilde{\mathscr{L}_{m-2}}^{\ast}\widetilde{\mathscr{L}_{m-2}}=\mathscr{L}_{m-2}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m-2}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}-1)\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\Delta-4((m-2)^{2}-1)-1)\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}\left(\,\cdot\,\right)\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}.

Therefore, we compute as previously

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u-2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}u\,\Delta\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)dx+2(m-1)\int_{\Omega}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(|x|^{2\alpha}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx.

(4.107)

Since
\mathscr{L}_{m}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}=\mathscr{L}_{m-2}^{\ast}\mathscr{L}_{m-2}
, we have by (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx-4((m-2)^{2}-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx.

(4.108)

Then, we have (compare with (
4.2
))

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)dx=-8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx-4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx

\displaystyle=-8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx-4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx+8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.109)

Then, we simply expand as in (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle=\left(8\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle-2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx+(m-1)^{2}\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.110)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle 4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)dx+\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx+4\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx=-\cancel{8\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx}-\cancel{4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx}

\displaystyle+8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+\left(\cancel{8\alpha^{2}}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+\cancel{4\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\,dx}

\displaystyle-2(m-1)(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx+(m-1)^{2}\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha-2(m-1)\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+(m-1)^{2}\left(4(m-1)\alpha-4\alpha^{2}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.111)

By (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+8(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle-4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+\left(16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha-2(m-1)\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+(m-1)^{2}\left(4(m-1)\alpha-4\alpha^{2}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.112)

Now, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}u\Delta u\,dx=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx-2(\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx-2(\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

(4.113)

while (
4.2
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)dx=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx+(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.114)

Therefore, we have by (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.114
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\left(4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+\bigg\{16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha-2(m-1)\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)-8(m-1)\alpha(\alpha-1)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle+\bigg\{(m-1)^{2}\left(4(m-1)\alpha-4\alpha^{2}\right)-4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)\bigg\}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.115)

Finally, using (
4.2
), since

\displaystyle(m-1)^{2}\left(4(m-1)\alpha-4\alpha^{2}\right)-4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)(\alpha-1)(2\alpha-1)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha)\bigg\{16(m-1)\alpha^{2}-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha-2(m-1)\left(4\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)\alpha\right)-8(m-1)\alpha(\alpha-1)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=4(m-1)\left(2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha-m+3\right),

we get by (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\left(4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)+4(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\left(2\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-3)\alpha-m+3\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.116)

We would be finished it it were not for this tangential part of the gradient. Therefore, using identity (
4.2
), we get

\displaystyle-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{(\partial_{\theta}u)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}u\,\Delta u\,dx+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+8(m-1)\alpha(\alpha-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx=4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha-2}u\,\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-8(m-1)\alpha(\alpha-1)^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx,

(4.117)

and (
4.2
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\left(4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)+8(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\Delta u\,dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\left(-2\alpha^{3}+6\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-5)\alpha-m+3\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.118)

Then, we have

\displaystyle-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\Delta u\,dx=-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\geq-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+4(m-1)^{3}(m-3)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.119)

On the other hand, we have (provided that
(m-2)^{2}-1+2(m-1)\alpha>0
(which is always true for
m\geq 2
and
\alpha>1
) by (
4.103
)

\displaystyle\left(4((m-2)^{2}-1)+8(m-1)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\geq 4(m-1)\left(m-3+2\alpha\right)(\alpha-1)^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.120)

Since

\displaystyle 4(m-1)\left(m-3+2\alpha\right)(\alpha-1)^{2}+4(m-1)\left(-2\alpha^{3}+6\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-5)\alpha-m+3\right)

\displaystyle-4(m-1)^{2}\alpha^{2}+4(m-1)^{3}(m-3)\alpha=4\,m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha,

(4.121)

we deduce by (
4.2
), (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.2
) that

\displaystyle 4\,m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.122)

Therefore, for all
m>3
and for all
\alpha>0
we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq\frac{1}{4\,m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u-2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\right)^{2}dx.

(4.123)

We also deduce that for all
m>3
and for all
\alpha>0

\displaystyle 4(m-1)(m-3+2\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\leq\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\left(4(m-1)(m-3+2\alpha)(\alpha-1)^{2}-4m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left(\frac{(m-3+2\alpha)(\alpha-1)^{2}}{m(m-1)(m-3)\alpha}-1\right)_{+}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx,

(4.124)

which shows that we have in particular that for all
m>3
and
\alpha>0

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\leq\left(\frac{1}{4(m-1)(m-3+2\alpha)}+\frac{(\alpha-1)^{2}}{4m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.125)

Furthermore, notice the two inequalities (
4.123
) and (
4.125
) show that for all
m>3
and
\alpha>0
, there exists
C_{m,\alpha}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx\leq C_{m,\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u-2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.126)

Finally, an integration by parts and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\Delta u\,dx+2(1-\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx-2(m-1)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}dx+2(1-\alpha)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\frac{u}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle+(m-1)^{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx+\left((m-1)^{2}+(2m-(\alpha+4))(\alpha-1)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

(4.127)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx+\left(\frac{1}{4}-(\alpha^{2}-(2m-3)\alpha-m^{2}+4m-5)\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left(\frac{1}{4(m-1)(m-3+2\alpha)}+\frac{(\alpha-1)^{2}}{4m(m-1)^{2}(m-3)\alpha}\right)\left(\frac{1}{4}-(\alpha^{2}-(2m-3)\alpha-m^{2}+4m-5)\right)_{+}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.128)

Now, using (
4.2
), we deduce by (
4.123
) and (
4.128
)
that for all
m>3
and for all
\alpha>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq C_{m,\alpha}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx.

Since the inequality holds true for all
\alpha>0
, we deduce in particular that for all
0<\alpha<2
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx+\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx\leq C_{m,2-\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx.

(4.129)

Now, the case
1<m\leq 3
remains to be treated. In reality, we are mostly interested in the cases
m=2
and
m=3
. Since
(m-2)^{2}-1\leq 0
for
1\leq m\leq 3
, it is expected that the previous computation does not lead to the because additional terms mostly contribute negative to the coefficient in front of the weight
L^{2}
norm of
u
. Starting from (
4.2
), we rewrite thanks to (
4.2
)

\displaystyle-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\Delta u\,dx=-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx-8(m-1)^{2}\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot u\right)dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)^{3}\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=-4(m-1)\alpha\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}u\,\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\,dx+\left(8(m-1)^{2}\alpha(\alpha-1)+4(m-1)^{3}\alpha\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle\geq\left(8(m-1)^{2}\alpha(\alpha-1)+4(m-1)^{3}\alpha-\delta\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx-\frac{4(m-1)\alpha^{2}}{\delta}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx

(4.130)

for all
\delta>0
. Therefore, we deduce by (
4.2
), (
4.103
), and (
4.2
) that for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{4(m-1)\alpha^{2}}{\delta}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx\geq\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\bigg\{\left(4((m-2)^{2}-1)+8(m-1)\alpha\right)(\alpha-1)^{2}+8(m-1)^{2}\alpha(\alpha-1)+4(m-1)^{3}\alpha-\delta

\displaystyle+4(m-1)\left(-2\alpha^{3}+6\alpha^{2}+(m^{2}-2m-5)\alpha-m+3\right)\bigg\}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u+\frac{((m-2)^{2}-1)}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx+\left(4(m-1)^{2}\alpha(3\alpha+2(m-3))-\delta\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.131)

Therefore, for all
\alpha>2-\dfrac{2}{3}m
, taking

\displaystyle\delta=2(m-1)^{2}\alpha(3\alpha+2(m-3))

we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{2(m-1)^{2}\alpha(3\alpha+2(m-3))}+\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{(m-1)\alpha(3\alpha+2(m-3))^{2}}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=C_{2,m,\alpha}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\Delta u-2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{2}dx

(4.132)

Then, we have

\displaystyle 4(m-1)^{2}\alpha(3\alpha+2(m-3))-\left(4((m-2)^{2}-1)+8(m-1)\alpha\right)

\displaystyle=-4(m-1)(2\alpha^{3}-2(m+2)\alpha^{2}+2(-m^{2}+3m+1)\alpha+m-3)

an

\displaystyle 4\left((m-2)^{2}-1\right)+8(m-1)\alpha=4(m-1)\left(2\alpha+m-3\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle 4(m-1)\left(2\alpha+m-3\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\leq\left(1+\frac{4(m-1)\alpha^{2}}{\delta}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+4(m-1)(2\alpha^{3}-2(m+2)\alpha^{2}+2(-m^{2}+3m+1)\alpha+m-3+\delta)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(4.133)

Taking
\delta=1
, we deduce that (taking
\delta=1
) that for all
\alpha>\dfrac{3-m}{2}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx\leq\left(\frac{1+4(m-1)\alpha^{2}}{4(m-1)(2\alpha+m-3)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(2\alpha^{3}-2(m+2)\alpha^{2}+2(-m^{2}+3m+1)\alpha+m-2)_{+}}{2\alpha+m-3}C_{2,m,\alpha}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.134)

Since
1<m\leq 3
, we have

\displaystyle 2-\frac{2}{3}m>\frac{3-m}{2}.

Therefore, for all
\alpha>2-\dfrac{2}{3}m
, (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.2
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\left(1+\left(\frac{1}{4}-(\alpha^{2}-(2m-3)\alpha-m^{2}+4m-5)\right)_{+}C_{2,m,\alpha}\right)\int_{\Omega}|x|^{2\alpha}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}^{\ast}u\right)^{2}dx.

(4.135)

Therefore, using the inversion formula (
4.2
) and the inequalities (
4.2
) and (
4.135
), we deduce that for all
\alpha>2-\dfrac{2}{3}m
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx+\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq C_{m,\alpha}^{\prime}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{4-2\alpha}\left(\Delta u+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}u\right)^{2}dx

In particular, we deduce that for all
1<m\leq 3
and for all
0<\alpha<\dfrac{4}{3}m
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx+\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx\leq C_{m,2-\alpha}\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}dx,

(4.136)

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

5
First Minimisation Problem in Dimension 4

5.1
Statement of the Main Theorem

Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and consider the following minimisation problem:

\displaystyle\lambda=\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx:\;\,u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=1\right\}.

(5.1)

Thanks to the Poincaré inequality, there exists a constant
C(\Omega)<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}u^{2}dx\leq C(\Omega)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq C(\Omega)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx.

Now, notice that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u)^{2}dx=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{x_{i}}(\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u)\partial_{x_{j}}u\,dx=-\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{x_{j}}(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}u)\partial_{x_{j}}u\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(5.2)

Therefore, we finally deduce that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, the holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\right)dx\leq C(\Omega)\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(5.3)

Therefore, taking a minimising sequence
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{k}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\Delta u\right)^{2}dx\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\lambda,

we deduce by (
5.3
) that
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, which shows that there exists
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
such that up to a subsequence,
u_{k}\hookrightarrow u
weakly as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Thanks to the continuous Sobolev embedding
W^{2,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}(\Omega)
, and the compact embedding
W^{1,4}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
for all
p<\infty
, we deduce in particular that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|u_{k}-u\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,2}(\Omega)}=0.

In particular,

\displaystyle 1=\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{k}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(5.4)

Finally, Fatou’s Lemma shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u_{k})^{2}dx=\lambda.

(5.5)

Therefore,
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
satisfies the constraint (
5.4
), and (
5.5
) shows that
u
is a minimiser for our problem (
5.1
). Therefore,
u
is a solution of the system of equations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}u&=\frac{\lambda}{|x|^{4}}u\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega\\
\partial_{\nu}u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.6)

Integrating by parts, by deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega}u\,\Delta^{2}u\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=\lambda,

and (
2.19
) shows that
\lambda>0
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
a minimiser of (
5.1
), and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{2}.

(5.7)

Then, we have

\displaystyle\lambda>\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

(5.8)

Furthermore, the minimiser is a radial function
u(r)
, where
Y(r)=u(e^{r})
is a non-trivial solution of the ordinary differential equation

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(t)-4\,Y^{\prime\prime}(t)=\lambda\,Y(t)

such that
Y=Y^{\prime}=0
on
\partial[\log(a),\log(b)]
.

Proof.

The bound (
5.8
) holds thanks to the forthcoming Theorem
5.4
from next section. Furthermore, notice that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the bound

\displaystyle\left(n^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((n+2)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)<\lambda_{n}<\left(n^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((n+2)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

shows that
\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
provided that

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(9+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)>\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\left(4+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Making the change of variable
X=\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, we deduce that this inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle(1+X)(9+X)>4X(4+4X)=16X(X+1),

or
-1<X<\dfrac{3}{5}
, which finally gives the bound

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\pi\sqrt{\frac{5}{3}},

which is satisfied by assumption. Therefore, we conclude as in the proof of Theorem
2.7
that the solution is radial and given as previously.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

The argument implies that the lower bound

\displaystyle\frac{b}{a}\geq e^{\pi\sqrt{\frac{5}{3}}}=57.73\cdots

may be optimal to have a radial solution. It also suggests that (
5.7
) is far from optimal. It would be interesting to investigate whether our minimisation problem admits a non-radial minimiser for small enough conformal class.

We deduce the following corollary that we state as theorem.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{2}.

(5.9)

Then, for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(5.10)

5.2
Eigenvalue Estimates for the Associated Ordinary Differential Equation

Recall the the Laplacian on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
admits the following expansion in polar coordinates

\displaystyle\Delta=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{3}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Delta_{S^{3}}.

Furthermore, all function
u\in L^{2}(S^{3})
admits an expansion

\displaystyle u(\theta)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}Y_{n}(\theta),

for some
\left\{a_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}
,
where
Y_{n}
is the restriction of a homogeneous harmonic polynomial on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
of degree
n
(and the
\left\{Y_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are orthogonal in
L^{2}(S^{3})
equipped with a fixed weight). Furthermore,
Y_{n}
has eigenvalue
n(n+2)
for
-\Delta_{S^{3}}
. Therefore, if
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
, using in polar coordinates
(r,\theta)\in(0,\infty)\times S^{3}
, we have an expansion

\displaystyle u(r,\theta)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(r)Y_{n}(\theta),

and

\displaystyle\Delta u=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)=a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{3+n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}(r)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)=a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{6+n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle+\frac{2(3+n(n+2))+2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{6n(n+2)}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r)

\displaystyle=a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{6+n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{6+4n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{6n(n+2)}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r).

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}u

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\bigg(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{6+n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{6+4n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{6n(n+2)}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{r}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{3+n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}(r)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{n(n+2)}{r^{2}}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)\bigg)Y_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{6}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{3-2n(n+2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{3+2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{n^{2}(n+2)^{2}}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r)\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

In other words, we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{n(n+2)}(\Delta^{2})=\partial_{r}^{4}+\frac{6}{r}\partial_{r}^{3}+\frac{3-2n(n+2)}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}-\frac{3+2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}+\frac{n^{2}(n+2)^{2}}{r^{4}}.

Now, consider the following eigenvalue problem on some interval
[a,b]
, where
0<a<b<\infty

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{6}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{3-2n(n+2)}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{3+2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}+\frac{n^{2}(n+2)^{2}}{r^{4}}f=\frac{\lambda}{r^{4}}f\\
&f(a)=f(b)=f^{\prime}(a)=f^{\prime}(b)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(5.11)

where
\lambda\geq 0
.
Making the change of variable
f(r)=Y(\log(r))
, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{r}f(r)=\frac{1}{r}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{3}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{3}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{2}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{4}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{11}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r)).

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle r^{4}\left(f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{6}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{3-2n(n+2)}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{3+2n(n+2)}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}+\frac{n^{2}(n+2)^{2}}{r^{4}}f\right)

\displaystyle=Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\cancel{-6\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}}+11\,Y^{\prime\prime}-6\,Y^{\prime}+6\left(\cancel{Y^{\prime\prime\prime}}-3\,Y^{\prime\prime}+2\,Y^{\prime}\right)+(3-2n(n+2))\left(Y^{\prime\prime}-Y^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-(3+2n(n+2))Y^{\prime}+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}Y

\displaystyle=Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}-(4+2n(n+2))Y^{\prime\prime}+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}Y,

and (
5.11
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}-(4+2n(n+2))Y^{\prime\prime}+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}Y=\lambda\,Y\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,(\log(a),\log(b))\\
&Y(\log(a))=Y(\log(b))=Y^{\prime}(\log(a))=Y^{\prime}(\log(b)).\end{aligned}\right.

(5.12)

The characteristic polynomial of the equation is

\displaystyle P(X)=X^{4}-(4+2n(n+2))X^{2}+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}-\lambda,

which is a biquadratic polynomial! The discriminant of
P(\sqrt{X})
is given by

\displaystyle 4(2+n(n+2))^{2}-4n^{2}(n+2)^{2}+4\lambda=16n(n+2)+16+4\lambda=4(4(n+1)^{2}+\lambda),

which shows that the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=&&\sqrt{(n+1)^{2}+1+\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}\\
r_{2}&=-&&\sqrt{(n+1)^{2}+1+\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}\\
r_{3}&=&&\sqrt{(n+1)^{2}+1-\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}\\
r_{4}&=-&&\sqrt{(n+1)^{2}+1-\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.13)

Since
\lambda\geq 0
(by assumption), we have

\displaystyle(n+1)^{2}+1-\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}\leq 0

if and only if

\displaystyle(n+1)^{4}+2(n+1)^{2}+1\leq\lambda+4(n+1)^{2},

or

\displaystyle\lambda\geq((n+1)^{2}-1)^{2}.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
0<a<b<\infty
be such that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\max\left\{\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}},\frac{\log(1+\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2})}{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}\right\}

(5.14)

For all
\lambda>0
, consider the following linear differential equation on
I=(\log(a),\log(b))
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(t)-(4+2n(n+2))Y^{\prime\prime}(t)+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}Y(t)&=\lambda_{n}Y(t)\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,I\\
Y=Y^{\prime}&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial I.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.15)

There exists
\lambda>0
such that the system (
5.12
) admits a non-trivial solution
Y
. Furthermore, the minimal value
\lambda_{n}>0
satisfies the following estimate

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}>\left(n^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((n+2)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

(5.16)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n}>\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

(5.17)

Remark 5.5
.

To obtain a more symmetric result as in Theorem
2.5
, we can rewrite the estimate as

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}>\left(((n+1)-1)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(((n+1)+1)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Furthermore, the proof shows that the bound

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}<\left(n^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((n+2)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

(5.18)

holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
Y
be a non-trivial solution of the system. We need to distinguish three cases.

Case 1:

\lambda<((n+1)^{2}-1)^{2}
.

Notice that by assumption this case cannot happen for
n=0
, but our proof also works for
n=0
.

Then, the characteristic polynomial
P(X)=X^{4}-(4+2n(n+2))X^{2}+n^{2}(n+2)^{2}-\lambda
of the ordinary differential equation has four distinct real roots, and

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}\,e^{r_{1}\,t}+\mu_{2}\,e^{r_{2}\,t}+\mu_{3}\,e^{r_{3}\,t}+\mu_{4}\,e^{r_{4}\,t},

(5.19)

where
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4}
are given in (
5.13
). Also write

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=&&\lambda_{1}\\
r_{2}&=-&&\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}&=&&\lambda_{2}\\
r_{4}&=-&&\lambda_{2}\end{aligned}\right.,

(5.20)

where
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{((n+1)^{2}+1+\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}
and
\lambda_{2}=\sqrt{(n+1)^{2}+1-\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}}
. The boundary conditions are equivalent to

\displaystyle A\,\mu=\begin{pmatrix}a^{r_{1}}&a^{r_{2}}&a^{r_{3}}&a^{r_{4}}\\
b^{r_{1}}&b^{r_{2}}&b^{r_{3}}&b^{r_{4}}\\
r_{1}\,a^{r_{1}}&r_{2}\,a^{r_{2}}&r_{3}\,a^{r_{3}}&r_{4}\,a^{r_{4}}\\
r_{1}\,b^{r_{2}}&r_{2}\,b^{r_{2}}&r_{3}\,b^{r_{3}}&r_{4}\,b^{r_{4}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

We recover the same algebraic expression as in
Step 1
of Theorem
2.5
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}\right)-\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)-8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2},

and since
\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\neq 0
, and
\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\neq 0
, the rest of the proof carries on and shows that
Y=0
.

Case 2:

\lambda=((n+1)^{2}-1)^{2}
.

Then, the roots are
r_{3}=r_{4}=0
, and
r_{1}=\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}
,
r_{2}=-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}
. Therefore,
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}\,e^{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}+\mu_{2}e^{-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}\,t,

and the boundary conditions are equivalent to

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a^{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&a^{-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&1&\log(a)\\
b^{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&b^{-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&1&\log(b)\\
\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}\,a^{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}\,a^{-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&0&1\\
\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}\,b^{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}\,b^{-\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}&0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

(5.21)

We recognise the determinant of
Step 2
of Theorem
2.5
given in (
2.7
).
Therefore, the proof of Theorem (
2.5
) applies and shows that
\det(A)\neq 0
, which finally implies that
Y=0
.

Case 3:

\lambda>((n+1)^{2}-1)^{2}
.

Then, we have by (
5.13
)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=&&\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}+(n+1)^{2}+1}\\
r_{2}&=-&&\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}+(n+1)^{2}+1}\\
r_{3}&=&&i\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}-((n+1)^{2}+1)}\\
r_{4}&=-&&i\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}-((n+1)^{2}+1)}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.22)

Provided that
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}+(n+1)^{2}+1}
and
\lambda_{2}=\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}-((n+1)^{2}+1)}
, we deduce that there exists
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu_{3},\mu_{4}\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{\lambda_{1}\,t}+\mu_{2}e^{-\lambda_{1}\,t}+\mu_{3}e^{i\,\lambda_{2}\,t}+\mu_{4}e^{-i\,\lambda_{2})t}

(5.23)

we get thanks to (
2.34
) the system

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a^{\lambda_{1}}&a^{-\lambda_{1}}&a^{i\,\lambda_{2}}&a^{-i\,\lambda_{2}}\\
b^{\lambda_{1}}&b^{-\lambda_{1}}&b^{i\,\lambda_{2}}&b^{-i\,\lambda_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}\,a^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}\,a^{-\lambda_{1}}&i\,\lambda_{2}\,a^{i\,\lambda_{2}}&-i\,\lambda_{2}\,a^{-i\,\lambda_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}\,b^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}\,b^{-\lambda_{1}}&i\,\lambda_{2}\,b^{i\,\lambda_{2}}&-i\,\lambda_{2}\,b^{-i\,\lambda_{2}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

(5.24)

We recognise the determinant of
Step 3
of Theorem
2.5
, and following the exact same steps, we deduce by (
2.48
) that

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}=((n+1)^{2}+1)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}-1\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right).

Since
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{2((n+1)^{2}+1)+\lambda_{2}^{2}}
, we deduce that our equation is equivalent to (
2.48
) (with
n
replaced by
k
so that we do not use twice the same notations), provide that
m\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
are chosen such that

\displaystyle m^{2}+k^{2}=(n+1)^{2}+1

We can therefore choose
m=1
, and
k=n+1
. Therefore, the proof of Theorem
2.5
shows that provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq R_{1,n+1}=\max\left\{\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}},\frac{\log(1+\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2})}{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}},\frac{\log(1+\log(1+\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}))}{\sqrt{2(n+1)^{2}+2}}\right\},

(which is a decreasing function of
n
), we have
\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\pi
, or

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}-((n+1)^{2}+1)}>\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda+4(n+1)^{2}}-((n+1)^{2}+1)>\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and finally,

\displaystyle\lambda>\left((n+1)^{2}+1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)^{2}-4(n+1)^{2}=\left(n^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left((n+2)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right),

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

5.3
Second Estimate in Neck Regions

Before stating the main result, let us generalise
4.1
to all dimension.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
2\leq n<\infty
, let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set, and consider
u\in W^{1,n}_{0}(\Omega)
. Then, for all
\beta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\beta\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{n}}dx=-2\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}u\frac{x}{|x|^{n}}\cdot\nabla u\,dx,

(5.25)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{n}}dx\leq\frac{4}{\beta^{2}}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{\frac{n}{2}}}\cdot\nabla u\right)^{2}dx

(5.26)

Proof.

Since we have already proved this statement for
n=2
(notice that the proof works for all domain), we can assume that
n\geq 3
. Thanks to the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
for all
p<\infty
, we deduce that all integrals are finite. Since the Green’s function for the Laplacian in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is given (up to a constant) by
x\mapstochar\rightarrow|x|^{2-n}
,
we deduce that

\displaystyle\dive\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{n}}\right)=0\qquad\text{in}\;\,\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\left\{0\right\}).

Therefore, since
\beta>0
, we have
|x|^{\beta}\Delta|x|^{2-n}=0
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u}{|x|^{\frac{n}{2}}}\frac{x}{|x|^{\frac{n}{2}}}\cdot\nabla u\,dx=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\frac{x}{|x|^{n}}\cdot\nabla(u^{2})\,dx=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\dive\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{n}}\,u^{2}\right)dx=-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{\Omega}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{n}}dx,

and the inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
∎

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and let
0<\beta<\infty
. There exists
C_{\beta}<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, the following estimate hold:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx

(5.27)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}\right)dx\leq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(5.28)

Proof.

Part 1: The Scalar Change of Variable.

Let
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
. Then, thanks to the Sobolev embeddings
W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\hookrightarrow W^{1,4}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
, and
W^{1,4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
for all
p<\infty
, we deduce that for all
\beta>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}<\infty.

Assume that
0<\beta<2
, since the inequality is trivial for
\beta\geq 2
thanks to (
5.3
). Integrating by parts, we deduce by Lemma
5.6
that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta-2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta-2}u\,\Delta u\,dx+(2-\beta)\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta-2}u\,\Delta u\,dx-\frac{2(2-\beta)}{\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

Using Cauchy’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle-\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta-2}u\,\Delta u\,dx\leq\frac{2-\beta}{\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+\frac{4\beta}{2-\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}(\Delta u)^{2}dx,

and finally

\displaystyle\frac{2-\beta}{\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx+\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\frac{4\beta}{2-\beta}\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

Less precisely, for all
\beta>0
, since
\nabla u\in L^{4}(B(0,1))
by Sobolev embedding and
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
we have

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta-2}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{dx}{|x|^{4-2\beta}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla u|^{4}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{\beta}}\left(\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla u|^{4}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\left(\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}dx,

(5.29)

where we used the Poincaré inequality and (
5.1
)

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla u|^{4}dx\leq C\int_{B(0,1)}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx=C\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

Therefore, Lemma
5.6
shows that for all
\beta>0
, there holds:

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|x|^{\beta}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\leq C\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

(5.30)

Now, if
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, making a change of variable
x=b\,y
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}dx=\int_{B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{b^{-1}a}(0)}|y|^{\beta}\frac{|u(by)|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy=\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy,

where
\overline{u}\in W^{2,2}_{0}(B(0,1))
is the extension by
0
of
u(b\,\cdot\,):B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{b^{-1}a}(0)
. Using (
5.30
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy\leq C\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta\overline{u})^{2}dy=C\int_{B(0,1)}(\Delta u(by))^{2}b^{4}\,dy=C\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx,

and making the same change of variable for
\nabla u
, the result follows for the first component of the integral (
5.28
).

Part 2: The Inversion.
Making an inversion
x=a\frac{y}{|y|^{2}}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}dx=\int_{B(0,1)}|\overline{u}|^{2}\frac{|y|^{4}}{a^{4}}|y|^{\beta}\frac{a^{2}dy}{|y|^{8}}=\int_{B(0,1)}|y|^{\beta}\frac{|\overline{u}|^{2}}{|y|^{4}}dy,

and by conformal invariance of the Bilaplacian in dimension
4
, the two estimates follow from the computations of
Part 2
.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

For
0<\beta<2
, we have the more precise estimates

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}dx\leq\frac{4\beta^{2}}{(2-\beta)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}dx
