\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}dx\leq\frac{4\beta^{2}}{(2-\beta)^{2}}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}dx,

and

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}dx\leq\frac{4\beta}{2-\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}dx

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,1)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}dx\leq\frac{4\beta}{2-\beta}\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}dx.

Finally, we deduce the following result.

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{2}.

Then, for all
\beta>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\beta}>0
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq C_{\beta}\int_{\Omega}\left(\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}+\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)dx.

(5.31)

6
First Minimisation Problem in All Dimension

Recall that the Laplacian on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
admits the following expansion in polar coordinates

\displaystyle\Delta=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{m-1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Delta_{S^{m-1}},

(6.1)

where
\Delta_{S^{m-1}}
is the Laplacian on
S^{m-1}
. Thanks to classical results, all function
u\in L^{2}(S^{m-1})
admits an expansion

\displaystyle u(\theta)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}Y_{n}(\theta)

for some sequence
\left\{a_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}
, where
Y_{n}
is the restriction to
S^{m-1}
of a homogeneous harmonic polynomial of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
(and the
\left\{Y_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are orthogonal in
L^{2}(S^{m-1})
. Furthermore,
Y_{n}
has eigenvalue
n(n+m-2)
. As we have already treated the case
m=2
, we can assume that
m\geq 3
. Now, if
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
, using polar coordinates
(r,\theta)\in(0,\infty)\times S^{m-1}
, we obtain an expansion

\displaystyle u(r,\theta)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(r)Y_{n}(\theta),

and

\displaystyle\Delta u=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

Then, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)=a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{m-1+n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle+\frac{2n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}(r),

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)=a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle-\frac{2(m-1)+n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2(m-1)+4n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{6n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r).

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}u=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\bigg(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{2(m-1)+n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2(m-1)+4n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle-\frac{6n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}a_{n}(r)

\displaystyle+\frac{m-1}{r}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)-\frac{m-1+n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{2n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}(r)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{m-1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}(r)\right)\bigg)Y_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

(6.2)

In other words, we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{n(n+m-2)}(\Delta^{2})=\partial_{r}^{4}+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}\partial_{r}^{3}+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}.

(6.3)

Now, consider the following eigenvalue problem on some interval
[a,b]
, where
0<a<b<\infty

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}\\
&-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}f=\frac{\lambda}{r^{4}}f\\
&f(a)=f(b)=f^{\prime}(a)=f^{\prime}(b)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(6.4)

where
\lambda\geq 0
.
Making the change of variable
f(r)=Y(\log(r))
, we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{r}f(r)=\frac{1}{r}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{2}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{1}{r^{2}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{3}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{3}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{2}{r^{3}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r))\\
&\partial_{r}^{4}f(r)=\frac{1}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}(\log(r))+\frac{11}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))-\frac{6}{r^{4}}Y^{\prime}(\log(r)).\end{aligned}\right.

(6.5)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle r^{4}\left(f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}f\right)

\displaystyle=Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}-6\,Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+11\,Y^{\prime\prime}-6\,Y^{\prime}+2(m-1)\left(Y^{\prime\prime\prime}-3\,Y^{\prime\prime}+2\,Y^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\left((m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)\right)\left(Y^{\prime\prime}-Y^{\prime}\right)-\left((m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)\right)Y^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\left(n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)\right)Y

\displaystyle=Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+2(m-4)Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+((m-1)(m-9)+11-2n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle-2((m-1)(m-5)+3+(m-4)n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime}+(n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(m+n-2))Y.

(6.6)

Therefore, (
6.4
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+2(m-4)Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+((m-1)(m-9)+11-2n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime\prime}\\
&-2((m-1)(m-5)+3+(m-4)n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime}\\
&+(n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(m+n-2))Y=\lambda\,Y\\
&Y(\log(a))=Y(\log(b))=Y^{\prime}(\log(a))=Y^{\prime}(\log(b))=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.7)

The characteristic polynomial of this equation is given by

\displaystyle P(X)

\displaystyle=X^{4}+2(m-4)X^{3}+((m-1)(m-9)+11-2n(n+m-2))X^{2}

\displaystyle-2((m-1)(m-5)+3+(m-4)n(n+m-2))X+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)-\lambda.

Make the change of variable
X=Y-(m-4)/2
. Then we get

\displaystyle X^{2}=Y^{2}-(m-4)Y+\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle X^{3}=Y^{3}-\frac{3}{2}(m-4)Y^{2}+\frac{3}{4}(m-4)^{2}Y-\frac{1}{8}(m-4)^{3}

\displaystyle X^{4}=Y^{4}-2(m-4)Y^{3}+\frac{3}{2}(m-4)^{2}Y^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(m-4)^{3}Y+\frac{(m-4)^{4}}{16}.

Also, let us rewrite the coefficients

\displaystyle(m-1)(m-9)+11=m^{2}-10m+20=(m-5)^{2}-5

\displaystyle(m-1)(m-5)+3=m^{2}-6m+8=(m-2)(m-4).

We therefore rewrite

\displaystyle P(X)

\displaystyle=X^{4}+2(m-4)X^{3}+(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2))X^{2}-2(m-4)(m-2+n(n+m-2))X

\displaystyle+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)-\lambda.

Therefore, the coefficient in
Y
is

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(m-4)^{3}+\frac{3}{2}(m-4)^{3}-(m-4)(m^{2}-10\,m+20-\cancel{2n(n+m-2)})

\displaystyle-2(m-4)(m-2+\cancel{n(n+m-2)})

\displaystyle=(m-4)\left((m-4)^{2}-m^{2}+10\,m-20\right)-2(m-2)(m-4)=(m-4)(2m-4)-2(m-4)(m-2)=0.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle Q(Y)=P(Y-(m-4)/2)=Y^{4}+\frac{3}{2}(m-4)^{2}Y^{2}+\frac{(m-4)^{4}}{16}

\displaystyle+2(m-4)\left(-\frac{3}{2}(m-4)Y^{2}-\frac{1}{8}(m-4)^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2))\left(Y^{2}+\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}\right)

\displaystyle-2(m-4)(m-2+n(n+m-2))\left(-\frac{m-4}{2}\right)

\displaystyle+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)-\lambda

\displaystyle=Y^{4}+(m^{2}-10m+20-\frac{3}{2}(m-4)^{2}-2n(n+m-2))Y^{2}-\frac{3}{16}(m-4)^{4}

\displaystyle+\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}\left(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2)\right)+(m-4)^{2}(m-2+n(n+m-2))+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}

\displaystyle+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)-\lambda

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-\left(\frac{1}{2}m^{2}-2m+4+2n(n+m-2)\right)Y^{2}+\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-\lambda

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)\right)Y^{2}+\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-\lambda.

(6.8)

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{16}(m-4)^{4}+\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2))+(m-4)^{4}(m-2+n(n+m-2))

\displaystyle=\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{16}\left(-3m^{2}+\cancel{24m}-\cancel{48}+4m^{2}-\cancel{40m}+\cancel{80}-8n(n+m-2)+\cancel{16m}-\cancel{32}+16n(n+m-2)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{16}\left(m^{2}+8n(n+m-2)\right),

which shows that the constant term is

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}}{16}+\frac{1}{2}(m-4)^{2}n(n+m-2)+n^{2}(n+m-2)+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}}{16}+\frac{1}{2}(m-4)n(n+m-2)((m-4)+4)+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}}{16}+\frac{1}{2}m(m-4)n(n+m-2)+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}.

Once more, we find a biquadratic polynomial! The discriminant of
Q(\sqrt{Y})
is given by

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-4\left(\frac{1}{4}m(m-4)+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}+4\lambda

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}(m^{2}-4m+8)+2n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}(m^{2}-4m)+2n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}+4\lambda

\displaystyle=4((m^{2}-4m+4)+4n(n+m-2))+4\lambda

\displaystyle=4((m-2)^{2}+4n^{2}+4n(m-2))+4\lambda=4((2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda).

(6.9)

For
m=4
, we recover the formula
4(4(n+1)^{2}+\lambda)
. Therefore, the roots of
Q(\sqrt{Y})
are given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)\pm\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda},

and finally, the roots of
Q
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} s_{1}&=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
s_{2}&=-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
s_{3}&=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
s_{4}&=-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
\end{aligned}\right.

(6.10)

Finally, the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{3}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{4}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}((m-2)^{2}+4)+n(n+m-2)-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
\end{aligned}\right.

(6.11)

Taking
m=2
, we recover (
2.30
) (where
m=1
in this identity), whilst for
m=4
, we recover (
5.13
).
We can also rewrite the roots as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{3}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
r_{4}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}}\\
\end{aligned}\right.

(6.12)

Notice that there are complex roots if and only if

\displaystyle\lambda>\frac{1}{16}\left((2n+m-2)^{2}-4\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{16}(2n+m)^{2}(2n+m-4)^{2},

For all
m\geq 3
, this quantity is minimal for
n=0
, and we will prove the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
m>2
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<a<b<\infty
, and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>15\sqrt{2+\frac{3\pi}{2}(\pi+1)}.

Let
Y
be a non-trivial solution of the system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+2(m-4)Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+((m-1)(m-9)+11-2n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime\prime}\\
&-2((m-1)(m-5)+3+(m-4)n(n+m-2))Y^{\prime}\\
&+(n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(m+n-2))Y=\lambda_{n}\,Y\\
&Y(\log(a))=Y(\log(b))=Y^{\prime}(\log(a))=Y^{\prime}(\log(b)),\end{aligned}\right.

(6.13)

where
\lambda_{n}\geq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}>\frac{1}{16}(2n+m)^{2}(2n+m-4)^{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n}>\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Finally, provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\pi\sqrt{\frac{3m^{2}-16m+28+\sqrt{(3m^{2}-16m+28)^{2}+60(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}}{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}},

we have

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n}=\lambda_{0}.

Proof.

Let
Y
be a non-trivial solution of the system. As previously, we distinguish three cases.

Case 1:
\lambda<\dfrac{1}{16}((2n+m-2)^{2}-4)^{2}
.
Then, the characteristic polynomial

\displaystyle P(X)=X^{4}+2(m-4)X^{3}+((m-1)(m-9)+11-2n(n+m-2))X^{2}

\displaystyle-2((m-1)(m-5)+3+(m-4)n(n+m-2))X+n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)-\lambda

\displaystyle=\left(X+\frac{m-4}{2}\right)^{4}-\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)\right)\left(X+\frac{m-4}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-\lambda

of the ordinary differential equation has four distinct real roots, and

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}\,e^{r_{1}\,t}+\mu_{2}\,e^{r_{2}\,t}+\mu_{3}\,e^{r_{3}\,t}+\mu_{4}\,e^{r_{4}\,t},

(6.14)

where
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4}
are given in (
5.13
). Also write

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{1}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{2}\\
r_{4}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{2}\end{aligned}\right.,

(6.15)

where
\lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1+\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}
and
\lambda_{2}=\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1-\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}
. The boundary conditions are equivalent to

\displaystyle A\,\mu=\begin{pmatrix}a^{r_{1}}&a^{r_{2}}&a^{r_{3}}&a^{r_{4}}\\
b^{r_{1}}&b^{r_{2}}&b^{r_{3}}&b^{r_{4}}\\
r_{1}\,a^{r_{1}}&r_{2}\,a^{r_{2}}&r_{3}\,a^{r_{3}}&r_{4}\,a^{r_{4}}\\
r_{1}\,b^{r_{2}}&r_{2}\,b^{r_{2}}&r_{3}\,b^{r_{3}}&r_{4}\,b^{r_{4}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

We recover up to a
a^{-m+2}b^{-m+2}
factor the determinant appearing in the proof of Theorem
2.5
, and since
\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\neq 0
,
(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\neq 0
, the rest of the proof applies identically and we deduce that
Y=0
.

Case 2:
\lambda=\frac{1}{16}((2n+m-2)^{2}-4)^{2}
.

Then, the roots are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(2n+m-2)^{2}+2}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(2n+m-2)^{2}+2}\\
r_{3}&=r_{4}=-\frac{m-4}{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Write
\mu=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(2n+m-2)^{2}+2}
to simplify notations. Therefore,
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{\mu\,t}+\mu_{1}e^{-\mu\,t}+\mu_{3}e^{-\frac{m-4}{2}t}+\mu_{4}\,t\,e^{-\frac{m-4}{2}t}.

The boundary conditions are equivalent to

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu}&a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu}&a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}&\log(a)a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}\\
b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu}&b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu}&b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}&\log(b)b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}\\
\left(-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu\right)a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu}&\left(-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu\right)a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu}&-\frac{m-4}{2}a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}&\left(1-\frac{m-4}{2}\log(a)\right)a^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}\\
\left(-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu\right)b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}+\mu}&\left(-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu\right)b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}-\mu}&-\frac{m-4}{2}b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}&\left(1-\frac{m-4}{2}\log(b)\right)b^{-\frac{m-4}{2}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

If
A
is the matrix above, we get

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle={\left(a^{\mu}\mu\log\left(a\right)+b^{\mu}\mu\log\left(a\right)-a^{\mu}\mu\log\left(b\right)-b^{\mu}\mu\log\left(b\right)-2\,a^{\mu}+2\,b^{\mu}\right)}a^{-m-\mu+4}{\left(a^{\mu}-b^{\mu}\right)}b^{-m-\mu+4}\mu

\displaystyle=\left(2\,b^{\mu}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}-1\right)-\mu\,b^{\mu}\left(1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)a^{-m-\mu+4}{\left(a^{\mu}-b^{\mu}\right)}b^{-m-\mu+4}\mu

\displaystyle=a^{-m-\mu+4}{\left(a^{\mu}-b^{\mu}\right)}b^{-m+4}\mu\left(2\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}-1\right)-\mu\left(1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\mu}\right)^{\mu}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right).

Since
a\neq b
, we have
\det(A)=0
if and only if

\displaystyle 2\left((1+x)^{\mu}-1\right)-\mu\left(1+(1+x)^{\mu}\right)\log(1+x)=0.

where
x=\dfrac{b}{a}-1
. Thanks to the proof of Theorem
2.5
, for all
\alpha\geq\sqrt{2}
and
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left((1+x)^{\alpha}-1\right)-\alpha\left(1+(1+x)^{\alpha}\right)\log(1+x)<0,

Since
\mu=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}((2n+m-2)^{2}+2}\geq\sqrt{2}
, we deduce that
\det(A)>0
, which implies that
Y=0
. Therefore, only one possibility remains.

Case 3:
\lambda>\frac{1}{16}((2n+m-2)^{2}-4)^{2}
.
Then, the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}+\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}+\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1}\\
r_{3}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+i\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}-\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}-1}\\
r_{4}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-i\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}-\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}-1}.\end{aligned}\right.

If

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}+\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}+1}

(6.16)

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}=\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda}-\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}-1}.

(6.17)

Up to a
a^{-m+2}b^{-m+2}
factor, we recover the quantity from the proof of Theorem
2.5
. Therefore, following the exact same steps, we deduce that this identity is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}

(6.18)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{2\lambda_{1}}-1\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right).

This equation correspond to (
2.48
) for
m=1
and
n=n+\frac{m-2}{2}
. Notice that we need not assume
m
or
n
integer in the proof of Theorem
2.5
. In particular, the proof applies and shows that if

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\max\left\{\frac{15\sqrt{4+3\pi(\pi+1)}}{\sqrt{2+2\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}}},\frac{2\log\left(1+\sqrt{2+2\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}}\right)}{\sqrt{2+2\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}}}\right\},

which is in particular satisfied provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq 15\sqrt{2+\frac{3\pi}{2}(\pi+1)},

then the first non-trivial zero
\theta_{0}
of
\psi
is such that

\displaystyle\pi<\theta_{0}<2\pi,

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda_{n}}-\frac{1}{4}(2n+m-2)^{2}-1}=\lambda_{2}>\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

or

\displaystyle\sqrt{(2n+m-2)^{2}+\lambda_{n}}>\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}+1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and finally

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}

\displaystyle>\left(\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}+1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)^{2}-4\left(n+\frac{m-2}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(n+\frac{m}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\left(n+\frac{m-4}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Finally, the other inequality shows that

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}<\left(\left(n+\frac{m}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\left(n+\frac{m-4}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

For all
m\geq 3
, we see that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n}>\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right),

(6.19)

and—provided that
m\neq 2
—we have
\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{n}=\lambda_{0}
provided that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)<\left(\left(1+\frac{m}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-2)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Letting
X=\dfrac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, we deduce that this equation is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+4X^{2}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+4X^{2}\right)<\left(\frac{(m+2)^{2}}{4}+X^{2}\right)\left(\frac{(m-2)^{2}}{4}+X^{2}\right),

or

\displaystyle 15X^{4}+\left(m^{2}+(m-4)^{4}-\frac{(m+2)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}}{4}\right)X^{2}+\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}-(m+2)^{2}(m-2)^{2}}{16}<0,

that can also be rewritten as

\displaystyle 15X^{4}+\left(m^{2}+(m-4)^{4}-\frac{(m+2)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}}{4}\right)X^{2}-\frac{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}{4}<0.

Therefore, this inequality is satisfied if and only if

\displaystyle X^{2}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{30}\left(-\left(m^{2}+(m-4)^{2}-\frac{(m+2)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}}{4}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sqrt{\left(m^{2}+(m-4)^{2}-\frac{(m+2)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}}{4}\right)^{2}+15(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}{\frac{3}{2}m^{2}-8m+14+\sqrt{\left(\frac{3}{2}m^{2}-8m+14\right)^{2}+15(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}},

and finally, we get the condition

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}<\sqrt{\frac{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}{3m^{2}-16m+28}+\sqrt{(3m^{2}-16m+28)^{2}+60(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}},

and

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\pi\sqrt{\frac{3m^{2}-16m+28+\sqrt{(3m^{2}-16m+28)^{2}+60(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}}{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}},

which finally concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Let
0<a<b<\infty
, let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
, and consider the following minimisation problem:

\displaystyle\lambda=\min\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx:\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=1\right\}.

(6.20)

Since (
5.3
) is true for all
m\geq 2
, using the Sobolev embedding
W^{2,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-2}}(\Omega)
, if
m=3
, we get
W^{2,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{1,6}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C^{0}(\Omega)
(where the last embedding), for
m=4
, we have a compact embedding
W^{2,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
for all
p<\infty
, and for
m\geq 5
, we have a compact embedding
W^{2,2}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{\frac{2m}{m-4}}(\Omega)
, and since
\dfrac{2m}{m-4}>2
, we deduce that the condition

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{k}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=1

passes to the limit as
k\rightarrow\infty
, which implies that there exists a minimiser of (
6.20
) for all
m\geq 2
. It satisfies the following equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta^{2}u&=\frac{\lambda}{|x|^{4}}u\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega\\
\partial_{\nu}u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

Now, the previous Theorem
6.1
implies the following result.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
m\geq 3
and
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
be a minimiser of (
6.20
), and assume that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq 15\sqrt{2+\frac{3\pi}{2}(\pi+1)}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\lambda>\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right).

Furthermore, provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\pi\sqrt{\frac{3m^{2}-16m+28+\sqrt{(3m^{2}-16m+28)^{2}+60(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}}{(2m-1)((m-1)^{2}-2)}},

the minimiser is a radial function
u(r)
, where
Y(r)=u(e^{r})
is a non-trivial solution of the ordinary differential equation

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+2(m-4)Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+((m-5)^{2}-5)Y^{\prime\prime}-2(m-2)(m-4)Y^{\prime}=\lambda\,Y

such that
Y=Y^{\prime}=0
on
\partial[\log(a),\log(b)]
.

Finally, we deduce the following inequality for dimension
m\geq 3
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
m\geq 3
, let
0<a<b<\infty
, and let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Then, for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\left(\frac{m^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(6.21)

7
Second Eigenvalue Problem in All Dimension

Assume that
m\geq 3
, and let
0<a<b<\infty
, and
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
. We also need to obtain an estimate of the form

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\quad\forall u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega),

where
\lambda_{0}>0
is independent of
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)
. Notice however that since the weight is non-critical, we should get a constant uniformly bounded from below as the conformal class diverges to
\infty
. Indeed, the following result holds.

Theorem 7.1

(
[
12
]
)
.

Let
d\geq 3
, let
0<a<b<\infty
, and let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.Then, for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\left(\frac{d^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\left(\frac{(d-4)^{2}}{4}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx.

(7.1)

Furthermore, for all
d\geq 3
and for all
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)\subset W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq\mu_{1}(\Omega)\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx,

(7.2)

where

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(\Omega)>\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{25+\dfrac{104\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{36+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}\qquad&&\text{for}\;\,d=3\\
&\frac{9+\dfrac{10\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{\pi^{4}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{3+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}\qquad&&\text{for}\;\,d=4\\
&\frac{d^{2}(d-4)^{2}+((d-2)^{2}+4)\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(d-4)^{2}+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}\qquad&&\text{for}\;\,d\geq 5.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.

Consider the following minimisation problem

\displaystyle\mu=\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega}(\Delta u)^{2}dx:u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=1\right\}.

A similar proof shows that a minimiser
u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
exists, and verifies

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}u=-\frac{\mu}{|x|^{2}}\left(\Delta-2\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\right)u.

Recall that the Laplacian on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
is given in polar coordinates as

\displaystyle\Delta=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{m-1}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Delta_{S^{m-1}}.

Make an expansion of
u\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
in polar coordinates
(r,\theta)\in(0,\infty)\times S^{n-1}
as

\displaystyle u(r,\theta)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(r)Y_{n}(\theta),

where
{Y_{n}:S^{m-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(S^{m-1})
made of restrictions to
S^{m-1}
of homogeneous harmonic polynomials. Furthermore, we have for all
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle-\Delta_{S^{m-1}}Y_{n}=n(n+m-2)Y_{n}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\Delta u=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{1}{r}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

and by (
6
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}u

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}a_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}a_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}a_{n}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}\right)Y_{n}(\theta).

Finally, we will also need the formula (
6
)

\displaystyle\Pi_{n(n+m-2)}(\Delta^{2})=\partial_{r}^{4}+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}\partial_{r}^{3}+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}\partial_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)}{r^{3}}\partial_{r}+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2(m-4)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}.

We also have

\displaystyle\Pi_{n(n+m-2)}\left(\Delta-2\dfrac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\right)=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{m-3}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}.

Therefore, we are led to consider the following eigenvalue problem on the compact interval
[a,b]

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &f^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{2(m-1)}{r}f^{\prime\prime\prime}+\frac{(m-1)(m-3)-2n(n+m-2)+\mu}{r^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}\\
&-\frac{(m-1)(m-3)+2(m-3)n(n+m-2)-(m-3)\mu}{r^{3}}f^{\prime}\\
&+\frac{n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+(2(m-4)-\mu)n(n+m-2)}{r^{4}}f=0\\
&f(a)=f(b)=f^{\prime}(a)=f^{\prime}(b)=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(7.3)

where
\mu\geq 0
. Making the change of variable
f(r)=Y(\log(r))
, we get by (
6.5
)

\displaystyle r^{2}\left(f^{\prime\prime}+\frac{m-3}{r}f^{\prime}-\frac{n(n+m-2)}{r^{2}}f\right)=Y^{\prime\prime}(\log(r))+(m-4)Y^{\prime}(\log(r))-n(n+m-2)Y(\log(r)),

while (
6
) shows that the eigenvalue problem (
7.3
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Y^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}+2(m-4)Y^{\prime\prime\prime}+(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2)+\mu)Y^{\prime\prime}\\
&-(m-4)\left(2(m-2)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)Y^{\prime}\\
&+(n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+(2(m-4)-\mu)n(n+m-2))Y=0\\
&Y(\log(a))=Y(\log(b))=Y^{\prime}(\log(a))=Y^{\prime}(\log(b)).\end{aligned}\right.

(7.4)

The character polynomial of this equation is given by

\displaystyle P(X)

\displaystyle=X^{4}+2(m-4)X^{3}+(m^{2}-10m+20-2n(n+m-2)+\mu)X^{2}

\displaystyle-(m-4)\left(2(m-2)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)X+n^{2}(n+m-2)+(2(m-4)-\mu)n(n+m-2),

or more simply

\displaystyle P(X)

\displaystyle=X^{4}+2(m-4)X^{3}+(m^{2}-10m+20-2\lambda_{n}+\mu)X^{2}-(m-4)(2(m-2)+2\lambda_{n}-\mu)X

\displaystyle+\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}+2(m-4)-\mu).

In particular, we see that for the critical dimension
m=4
, we get a biquadratic equation. Consider the polynomial

\displaystyle R(X)=X^{2}+(m-4)X-n(n+m-2).

If
X=Y-\dfrac{(m-4)}{2}
, we get

\displaystyle R(Y)

\displaystyle=\left(Y^{2}-(m-4)Y+\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}\right)+(m-4)\left(Y-\frac{m-4}{2}\right)-n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle=Y^{2}-\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}-n(n+m-2)

Therefore, if
Q(Y)=P(X-(m-4)/2)
, we deduce by (
6
) that

\displaystyle Q(Y)

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)\right)Y^{2}+\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\mu\left(Y^{2}-\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}-n(n+m-2)\right)

\displaystyle=Y^{4}-\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)Y^{2}+\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\mu\left(\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right).

Thanks to (
6
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}-4\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}=4(2n+m-2)^{2}

Therefore, we deduce that the discriminant of
Q
is given by

\displaystyle D_{m}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}(m^{2}-4m+8)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-4\left(\frac{m(m-4)}{4}+n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}+\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)\right)\mu

\displaystyle=4(2n+m-2)^{2}-((m^{2}-4m+8)+4n(n+m-2))\mu+\mu^{2}+((m^{2}-8m+16)+4n(n+m-2))\mu

\displaystyle=4(2n+m-2)^{2}-4(m-2)\mu+\mu^{2}.

The discriminant of this quadratic polynomial in
\mu
is given by

\displaystyle 16(m-2)^{2}-16(2n+m-2)^{2}\leq 0

for all
m\geq 3
and
n\geq 0
. Therefore, this polynomial is always positive, which can also be seen directly since

\displaystyle 4(2n+m-2)^{2}-4(m-2)\mu+\mu^{2}=(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+4(2n+m-2)^{2}-4(m-2)^{2}

\displaystyle=(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+4(2n)(2n+2m-4)=(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2).

We deduce that the roots of
Q(\sqrt{Y})
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} s_{1}&=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu+\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}\right)\\
s_{2}&=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}((m-2)^{2}+4)+2n(n+m-2)-\mu-\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}\right),\end{aligned}\right.

and finally, the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}}\\
r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}}\\
r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}}\\
r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}}\end{aligned}\right.

(7.5)

Then, notice that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}>\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu

if and only if

\displaystyle(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)\geq\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{4}+2(m-2)^{2}+4+4n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}

\displaystyle+2((m-2)^{2}+4)n(n+m-2)-((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2))\mu+\mu^{2},

or

\displaystyle((m-2)^{2}-4(m-2)+4+4n(n+m-2))\mu

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}-2(m-2)^{2}+4+4n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}-4)n(n+m-2)

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)\right)\mu

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}-2\right)^{2}+4n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}-4)n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}-2+2n(n+m-2)\right)^{2},

and finally (unless
m=4
and
n=0
)

\displaystyle\mu\geq\frac{1}{4}\frac{\left((m-2)^{2}-4+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}=\frac{1}{4}\frac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}.

Therefore, assume that
(m,n)\neq(4,0)
in the following. Then we need to distinguish three cases as previously.

Case 1:
\mu<\dfrac{1}{4}\dfrac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}
.
Then, the characteristic polynomial admits four distinct real roots, and the solution
Y
to (
7.4
) is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{r_{1}t}+\mu_{2}e^{r_{2}t}+\mu_{3}e^{r_{3}t}+\mu_{4}e^{r_{4}t}

for some
\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
. Writing

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{1}\\
r_{2}=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{2}\\
r_{4}=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{2}\end{aligned}\right.,

if
A
is the boundary conditions matrix, we have by (
2.38
)

\displaystyle\det(A)=(ab)^{-(m-4)}\left((\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}}\right)-\left(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\right)-8\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\right)

which implies that
\det(A)\neq 0
, a contradiction.

Case 2:
\mu=\dfrac{1}{4}\dfrac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}
.
In this case, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}=\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\mu,

which implies that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu}=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{1}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu}=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{1}\\
r_{3}&=r_{4}=-\frac{m-4}{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Recall that by (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\det\begin{pmatrix}X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}&X^{\gamma}&\log(X)X^{\gamma}\\
Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}&Y^{\gamma}&\log(Y)Y^{\gamma}\\
\alpha X^{\alpha}&\beta X^{\beta}&\gamma X^{\gamma}&(1+\gamma\log(X))X^{\gamma}\\
\alpha Y^{\alpha}&\beta Y^{\beta}&\gamma Y^{\gamma}&(1+\gamma\log(Y))X^{\gamma}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=(\alpha-\beta)\left(X^{\alpha+\beta}Y^{2\gamma}+X^{2\gamma}Y^{\alpha+\beta}\right)+(\beta-\alpha)\left(X^{\alpha+\gamma}Y^{\beta+\gamma}+X^{\beta+\gamma}Y^{\alpha+\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\alpha(\beta-\gamma)+\gamma^{2}\right)X^{\alpha+\gamma}Y^{\beta+\gamma}\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)-\left(\beta(\alpha-\gamma)+\gamma^{2}\right)X^{\beta+\gamma}Y^{\alpha+\gamma}\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)

\displaystyle+\gamma\left(\alpha X^{\beta+\gamma}Y^{\alpha+\gamma}-\beta X^{\alpha+\gamma}Y^{\beta+\gamma}\right)\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right).

(7.6)

Taking now

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\alpha=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)}=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\lambda_{1}\\
&\beta=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)}=-\frac{m-4}{2}-\lambda_{1}\\
&\gamma=r_{3}=-\frac{m-4}{2}\end{aligned}\right.

(7.7)

if
A
is the boundary conditions matrix, we get (if
X=a
and
Y=b
)

\displaystyle\det(A)=2\lambda_{1}\left(X^{4-m}Y^{4-m}+X^{4-m}Y^{4-m}\right)-2\lambda_{1}\left(X^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}+X^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\left(-\cancel{\frac{m-4}{2}}+\lambda_{1}\right)(-\lambda_{1})+\cancel{\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}}\right)X^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\left(-\cancel{\frac{m-4}{2}}-\lambda_{1}\right)\lambda_{1}+\cancel{\frac{(m-4)^{2}}{4}}\right)\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)X^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{m-4}{2}\left(\left(-\cancel{\frac{m-4}{2}}+\cancel{\lambda_{1}}\right)X^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}-\left(-\cancel{\frac{m-4}{2}}-\cancel{\lambda_{1}}\right)X^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)

\displaystyle=4\lambda_{1}X^{4-m}Y^{4-m}-2\lambda_{1}\left(X^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}+X^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(X^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}-X^{4-m-\lambda_{1}}Y^{4-m+\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{Y}{X}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}(ab)^{4-m}\left(4-2\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-2\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}+\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)<0,

(7.8)

where we used the inequality proven after (
2.7
).
Therefore, this case is also excluded, and we can finally move to the analysis of the last case.

Case 3:
\mu>\dfrac{1}{4}\dfrac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}
.

Then, the roots of the characteristic polynomial
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}+\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}}\\
r_{2}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}+\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}}\\
r_{3}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+i\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}-\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}-1-n(n+m-2)+\frac{\mu}{2}}\\
r_{4}&=-\frac{m-4}{2}+i\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}-\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}-1-n(n+m-2)+\frac{\mu}{2}}.\end{aligned}\right.

Recalling that

\displaystyle\begin{vmatrix}X^{\alpha}&X^{\beta}&X^{\gamma}&X^{\delta}\\
Y^{\alpha}&Y^{\beta}&Y^{\gamma}&Y^{\delta}\\
\alpha\,X^{\alpha}&\beta\,X^{\beta}&\gamma\,X^{\gamma}&\delta\,X^{\delta}\\
\alpha\,Y^{\alpha}&\beta\,Y^{\beta}&\gamma\,Y^{\gamma}&\delta\,Y^{\delta}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=(\beta-\alpha)(\gamma-\delta)X^{\alpha+\beta}Y^{\gamma+\delta}+(\gamma-\alpha)(\delta-\beta)X^{\alpha+\gamma}Y^{\beta+\delta}

\displaystyle+(\delta-\alpha)(\beta-\gamma)X^{\alpha+\delta}Y^{\beta+\gamma}+(\beta-\gamma)(\delta-\alpha)X^{\beta+\gamma}Y^{\alpha+\delta}

\displaystyle+(\beta-\delta)(\alpha-\gamma)X^{\beta+\delta}Y^{\alpha+\gamma}+(\delta-\gamma)(\alpha-\beta)X^{\gamma+\delta}Y^{\alpha+\beta},

we deduce by the computations preceding (
2.46
) that the boundary conditions matrix satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\det(A)=2i\,(ab)^{4-m}\left(\mathrm{Im}\,\left(\zeta^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{\overline{\zeta}}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\overline{\zeta}}\right)\right)-4\,\mathrm{Re}\,(\zeta)\mathrm{Im}\,(\zeta)\right)

where
\zeta=\lambda_{1}+i\,\lambda_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \lambda_{1}&=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}+\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}}\\
\lambda_{2}&=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}-\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}-1-n(n+m-2)+\frac{\mu}{2}}\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, following the exact same steps before (
2.47
), we deduce that

\displaystyle 2\left(\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\cos\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\right)\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}-\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\sin\left(\lambda_{2}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=0.

(7.9)

We have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu

\displaystyle(\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left((\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+1+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)\right)\mu-\frac{1}{16}\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}.

Therefore, the equation becomes if
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)+\lambda_{2}^{2}}
, noticing that
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
as
\mu\geq\dfrac{1}{4}\dfrac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)}
, provided that

\displaystyle 2p^{2}+2q^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu,

assuming without loss of generality that

\displaystyle\mu\leq\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2n(n+m-2),

we can take
p=1
and

\displaystyle q=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}(m-2)^{2}+n(n+m-2)-\frac{\mu}{2}}

since
n
(
p
in the current notations) need not be integer in this part of the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we deduce that provided that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{5\pi}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu}},

(7.10)

the first positive zero
\lambda_{2}^{\ast}>0
of the equation (
7.9
) satisfies the inequality

\displaystyle\pi<\frac{\lambda_{2}^{\ast}}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}<2\pi.

(7.11)

In particular, we get

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}-\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}-2-2n(n+m-2)+\mu>\frac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

or

\displaystyle(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle>\frac{4\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)^{2},

that we rewrite

\displaystyle\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)\mu>\frac{1}{4}\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\frac{4\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and finally

\displaystyle\mu>\frac{\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}+\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(7.12)

Now, as previously, we need to express the bound (
7.10
) into a condition that only depends on
m
and
n
. On the other hand, the estimate (
7.11
) shows that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)}-\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}-2-2n(n+m-2)+\mu<\frac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

which implies that

\displaystyle(\mu-2(m-2))^{2}+16n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle<\frac{64\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)-\mu\right)^{2},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)+\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{4}\left(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\frac{64\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and finally

\displaystyle\mu<\frac{(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2))^{2}+\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)\dfrac{64\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{256\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+\dfrac{64\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}.

(7.13)

Let
X=\dfrac{4\pi}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
, and let us estimate

\displaystyle c(X)=\frac{1}{2}(m-2)^{2}+2+2n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle-\frac{(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2))^{2}+4\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)X^{2}+X^{4}}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+4X^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+4X^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(2\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2))\left((m-4)^{2}+4n(n+m-2)+X^{2})\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(m(m-4)+4n(n+m-2))^{2}-4\left((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2)\right)X^{2}-X^{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+4X^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(2((m-2)^{2}+4)(m-4)^{2}+8\left((m-2)^{2}+(m-4)^{2}+4\right)n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle+32n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}-2((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2))X^{2}-m^{2}(m-4)^{2}-8m(m-4)n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle-16n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}-X^{4}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(m-4)^{2}+16n(n+m-2)+4X^{2}}\bigg((m-4)^{4}+8((m-4)^{2}+4)n(n+m-2)

\displaystyle+16n^{2}(n+m-2)^{2}-2((m-2)^{2}+4+4n(n+m-2))X^{2}-X^{4}\bigg),

where we used

\displaystyle 8((m-2)^{2}+(m-4)^{2}+4)-8m(m-4)

\displaystyle=8((2m^{2}-12m+20)-m^{2}+4m)=8(m^{2}-8m+24)

\displaystyle=8((m-4)^{2}+8)

\displaystyle 2((m-2)^{2}+4)-m^{2}

\displaystyle=m^{2}-8m+16=(m-4)^{2}.

At
X=0
, we have
C(X)\geq 0
, and
C(X)=0
if and only if
m=4
and
n=0
, but this case is excluded from our analysis. Therefore, we get the following condition on
X

\displaystyle X<\sqrt{(m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n}+\sqrt{((m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n})^{2}+(m-4)^{4}+8((m-4)^{2}+4)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}}},

where we wrote for simplicity
\lambda_{n}=\lambda_{n}
. Finally, we get the condition
or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)>\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{(m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n}+\sqrt{((m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n})^{2}+(m-4)^{4}+8((m-4)^{2}+4)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}}}}.

(7.14)

Assume that this bound holds on the conformal class. Then, (
7.10
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{X}\geq\frac{5}{\sqrt{c(X)}},

that we rewrite as

\displaystyle 25X^{2}\leq c(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(m-4)^{2}+16\lambda_{n}+4X^{2}}\bigg((m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}

\displaystyle+16\lambda_{n}^{2}-2\big((m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n}\big)X^{2}-X^{4}\bigg),

or

\displaystyle 25(4(m-4)^{2}X^{2}+16\lambda_{n}X^{2}+4X^{4})

\displaystyle\leq(m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}-2\big((m-2)^{2}+4+4\lambda_{n}\big)X^{2}

\displaystyle-X^{4}

that we finally rewrite as

\displaystyle 2\big(50(m-4)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}+4+204\lambda_{n}\big)X^{2}+101X^{4}\leq(m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2},

and finally gives us the estimate

\displaystyle X^{2}\leq-\big(50(m-4)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}+4+204\lambda_{n}\big)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\big(50(m-4)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}+4+204\lambda_{n}\big)^{2}+(m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}}.

Therefore, the condition on the conformal class becomes

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq

(7.15)

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\sqrt{\big(50(m-4)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}+4+204\lambda_{n}\big)+\sqrt{\big(50(m-4)^{2}+(m-2)^{2}42+204\lambda_{n}\big)^{2}+(m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}}}}{\sqrt{(m-4)^{4}+8\big((m-4)^{2}+4\big)\lambda_{n}+16\lambda_{n}^{2}}}.

The important point is that this number is bounded in
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Finally, we treat the remaining case
m=4
,
n=0
.

Final Case:
m=4
and
n=0
. Then, the roots of the characteristic polynomial are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=&&\sqrt{2-\frac{\mu}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-4)^{4}}}&=&&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{4-\mu+|4-\mu|}\\
r_{2}&=-&&\sqrt{2-\frac{\mu}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-4)^{4}}}&=-&&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{4-\mu+|4-\mu|}\\
r_{3}&=&&\sqrt{2-\frac{\mu}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-4)^{4}}}&=&&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{4-\mu-|4-\mu|}\\
r_{4}&=-&&\sqrt{2-\frac{\mu}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{(\mu-4)^{4}}}&=-&&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{4-\mu-|4-\mu|}.\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, we distinguish three cases.

Sub-case 1:
\mu<4
.
Then

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=&&\sqrt{4-\mu}\\
r_{2}&=-&&\sqrt{4-\mu}\\
r_{3}&=&&0\\
r_{4}&=&&0.\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore,
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}e^{\sqrt{4-\mu}\,t}+\mu_{2}e^{-\sqrt{4-\mu}\,t}+\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}t.

Therefore, we have thanks to the boundary conditions if
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{4-\mu}

\displaystyle A\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}a^{\lambda_{1}}&a^{-\lambda_{1}}&1&\log(a)\\
b^{\lambda_{1}}&b^{-\lambda_{1}}&1&\log(b)\\
\lambda_{1}a^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}a^{-\lambda_{1}}&0&1\\
\lambda_{1}b^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}b^{-\lambda_{1}}&0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

As
Y\neq 0
, we have
\det(A)=0
. Now, expanding on the third column, we compute

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=-\begin{vmatrix}b^{\lambda_{1}}&b^{-\lambda_{1}}&\log(b)\\
\lambda_{1}a^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}a^{-\lambda_{1}}&1\\
\lambda_{1}b^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}b^{-\lambda_{1}}&1\end{vmatrix}+\begin{vmatrix}a^{\lambda_{1}}&a^{-\lambda_{1}}&\log(a)\\
\lambda_{1}a^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}a^{-\lambda_{1}}&1\\
\lambda_{1}b^{\lambda_{1}}&-\lambda_{1}b^{-\lambda_{1}}&1\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\left(-\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\log(b)+\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}\log(b)-\lambda_{1}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}+\lambda_{1}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\lambda_{1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\log(a)+\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}\log(a)-\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{1}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle=-4\lambda_{1}+2\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)-\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{1}\left(4-2\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)+\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right).

However, as
\lambda_{1}>0
, this function of
0<a<b<\infty
never vanished thanks to the analysis that follows (
2.7
). Therefore, we get a contradiction since
Y\neq 0
by hypothesis. Let us now move to the second alternative.

Sub-case 2:
\mu=4
.
Then, all roots are equal to
0
, which shows that

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}\,t+\mu_{3}\,t^{2}+\mu_{4}\,t^{3}.

Writing to simplify
X=\log(a)
and
Y=\log(b)
, the boundary conditions show that

\displaystyle A\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&X&X^{2}&X^{3}\\
1&Y&Y^{2}&Y^{3}\\
0&1&2X&3X^{2}\\
0&1&2Y&3Y^{2}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

Expanding on the first column, we get

\displaystyle\det(A)=\begin{pmatrix}Y&Y^{2}&Y^{3}\\
1&2X&3X^{2}\\
1&2Y&3Y^{2}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}X&X^{2}&X^{3}\\
1&2X&3X^{2}\\
1&2Y&3Y^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=6XY^{3}+3X^{2}Y^{2}+2Y^{4}-2XY^{3}-3Y^{4}-6X^{2}Y^{2}-\left(6X^{2}Y^{2}+3X^{4}+2X^{3}Y-2X^{4}-3X^{2}Y^{2}-6X^{3}Y\right)

\displaystyle=-(X^{4}+Y^{4})+4XY(X^{2}+Y^{2})-6X^{2}Y^{2}=-\left(X-Y\right)^{4}=-\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\neq 0.

Therefore, we have
Y=0
.

Sub-case 3:
\mu>4
.
Then, the roots of
P
are given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} r_{1}&=r_{2}&&=0\\
r_{3}&=&&i\sqrt{\mu-4}\\
r_{4}&=-&&i\sqrt{\mu-4}\end{aligned}\right.

and
Y
is given by

\displaystyle Y(t)=\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}t+\mu_{3}e^{i\lambda_{1}t}+\mu_{4}e^{-i\lambda_{1}t}.

where
\lambda_{1}=\sqrt{\mu-4}
. The boundary conditions imply that

\displaystyle A\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&\log(a)&a^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&a^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
1&\log(b)&b^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&b^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
0&1&i\,\lambda_{1}a^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}a^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
0&1&i\,\lambda_{1}b^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}b^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mu_{1}\\
\mu_{2}\\
\mu_{3}\\
\mu_{4}\end{pmatrix}=0.

We have

\displaystyle\det(A)

\displaystyle=\begin{vmatrix}\log(b)&b^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&b^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
1&i\,\lambda_{1}a^{i\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}a^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
1&i\,\lambda_{1}b^{i\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}b^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\end{vmatrix}-\begin{vmatrix}\log(a)&a^{i\lambda_{1}}&a^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
1&i\,\lambda_{1}a^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}a^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\\
1&i\,\lambda_{1}b^{i\,\lambda_{1}}&-i\,\lambda_{1}b^{-i\,\lambda_{1}}\end{vmatrix}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{\lambda_{1}}\log(b)-i\,\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\lambda_{1}}+i\,\lambda_{1}-i\,\lambda_{1}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}+i\,\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}\log(b)

\displaystyle-\left(\lambda_{1}^{2}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}\log(a)-i\,\lambda_{1}+i\,\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}-i\,\lambda_{1}+i\,\lambda_{1}\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}-\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=4\,i\,\lambda_{1}-2\,i\,\lambda_{1}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}+\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}\right)-\lambda_{1}^{2}\left(\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}-\bigg(\frac{a}{b}\bigg)^{i\,\lambda_{1}}\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)

\displaystyle=4\,i\,\lambda_{1}-4\,i\,\lambda_{1}\cos\left(\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)-2\,i\,\lambda_{1}^{2}\sin\left(\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)

\displaystyle=2\,i\,\lambda_{1}\left(2\left(1-\cos\left(\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\right)-\lambda_{1}\sin\left(\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right).

Therefore, writing
\theta=\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)
, we are brought to the study of the first positive zero of the function

\displaystyle f(\theta)=2(1-\cos(\theta))-\theta\sin(\theta),

which is elementary. We have
f(0)=0
, and

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\sin(\theta)-\theta\cos(\theta)

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\theta\sin(\theta).

Therefore,
f^{\prime\prime}(\theta)>0
for all
0<\theta<\pi
and
f^{\prime\prime}(\theta)<0
for all
\pi<\theta<2\pi
. The function
f^{\prime}
is strictly increasing on
[0,\pi]
and strictly decreasing on
[\pi,2\pi]
. We have
f^{\prime}(\pi)=\pi>0
, which implies in particular that
f
is strictly decreasing on
[0,\pi]
. Since
f^{\prime}(2\pi)=-2\pi<0
, there exists
\pi<\theta_{0}<2\pi
such that
f^{\prime}(\theta)>0
for all
0<\theta<\theta_{0}
and
f^{\prime}(\theta)<0
for all
\theta_{0}<\theta<2\pi
. Finally, since
f(2\pi)=0
, we deduce that
f(\theta)>0
for all
0<\theta<2\pi
, and the first non-trivial zero
\theta_{1}
of the equation
\det(A)=0
is
2\pi
. Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)=2\pi,

or

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mu-4}=\frac{2\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

which finally gives us

\displaystyle\mu=4+\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

We can now move to the global bound on the eigenvalues and the computation of the smallest eigenvalue for large enough conformal class. Since the case
d=4
is special, we start by this case.

Step 2: Lower bound on all eigenvalues in dimension
\mathbf{4}
.

We have

\displaystyle\mu_{0}=4+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)},

and for all
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mu_{n}>\frac{16\lambda_{n}^{2}+\left(2+\lambda_{n}\right)\dfrac{32\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{16\lambda_{n}+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}=\frac{\left(4\lambda_{n}+4X^{2}\right)^{2}+64X^{2}}{16\lambda_{n}+16X^{2}}=\lambda_{n}+X^{2}+\dfrac{4X^{2}}{\lambda_{n}+X^{2}},

where
\lambda_{n}=n(n+2)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and where we denoted
X=\dfrac{\pi}{\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
for simplicity. Let
\alpha>0
and

\displaystyle f(t)=t+\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{t+\alpha^{2}}.

We have

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)=1-\frac{4\alpha^{2}}{(t+\alpha^{2})^{2}}>0

if and only if

\displaystyle t>2\alpha-\alpha^{2}=\alpha(2-\alpha).

Since
\alpha(2-\alpha)\leq 1<\lambda_{1}=3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\geq 1}\left(\lambda_{n}+X^{2}+\dfrac{4X^{2}}{\lambda_{n}+X^{2}}\right)=\lambda_{1}+X^{2}+\frac{4X^{2}}{\lambda_{1}+X^{2}}=3+X^{2}+\frac{4X^{2}}{3+X^{2}}.

Then, we have for all
X\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle 4+4X^{2}\geq 3+X^{2}+\frac{4X^{2}}{3+X^{2}}

since this inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle 3+10X^{2}+3X^{4}\geq 4X^{2}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{n}>3+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{\pi^{2}+3\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}.

Step 3: Lower bound on all eigenvalues in dimension
\mathbf{3}
.

Thanks to (
7.12
), we have

\displaystyle\mu>\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{n}

\displaystyle=\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\{\frac{(4n(n+1)-3)^{2}+(5+4n(n+1))\dfrac{8\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{4+16n(n+1)+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}=\frac{25+\dfrac{104\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}+\dfrac{16\pi^{4}}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}{36+\dfrac{16\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}}

if the conformal class is large enough. Indeed, let
\alpha=\dfrac{4\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)}
and
t=4n(n+1)
, and introduce on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
the function

\displaystyle f(t)=\frac{(t-3)^{2}+2\alpha(5+t)+\alpha^{2}}{1+\alpha+t}=9+\alpha+t-\frac{16t}{1+\alpha+t}.

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)=1-\frac{16(1+\alpha)}{(1+\alpha+t)^{2}}.

Therefore,
f
is strictly decreasing on
[0,4\sqrt{1+\alpha}-1-\alpha]
and strictly increasing on
[4\sqrt{1+\alpha}-1-\alpha,\infty[
provided that
4\sqrt{1+\alpha}\geq 1+\alpha
, or

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}-14\alpha-15\leq 0,

which is verified if and only if
\alpha\leq 15
. Since for all
n\geq 1
, we have
4n(n+1)\geq 8>4\sqrt{1+\alpha}-1-\alpha
for all
\alpha>0
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f(4n(n+1))=\min\left\{f(0),f(8)\right\}=\min\left\{9+\alpha,\frac{25+26\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{9+\alpha}\right\}.

Since
(9+\alpha)^{2}-(25+26\alpha+\alpha^{2})=56-8\alpha=8(7-\alpha)
, we deduce that the condition
\alpha\leq 7
, or

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{7}}

implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f(4n(n+1))=\min\left\{f(0),f(8)\right\}=\frac{25+26\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{9+\alpha}.

Step 4: Lower bound on all eigenvalues in dimension
m\geq 5
.

Introduce on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
the function

\displaystyle f(t)=\frac{(m(m-4)+t)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}+4+t)\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+t+\alpha}=m^{2}+\alpha+t-\frac{16t}{(m-4)^{2}+t+\alpha},

where
t
stands for
4n(n+m-2)
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
).
We have

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)=1-\frac{16((m-4)^{2}+\alpha)}{((m-4)^{2}+t+\alpha)^{2}}.

For
m\geq 8
, the function
f
is strictly increasing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, which shows that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f(4n(n+m-2))=f(0)=\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}+4)\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+\alpha}.

If
5\leq m\leq 7
, since
4n(n+m-2)>4\sqrt{(m-4)^{2}+\alpha}-(m-4)^{2}-\alpha
for all
n\geq 1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f(4n(n+m-2))=\min\left\{f(0),f(4(m-1))\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}+4)\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+\alpha},\frac{(m+2)^{2}(m-2)^{2}+2(m+2)^{2}\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)+\alpha}\right\}.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{(m+2)^{2}(m-2)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)}-\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}}=\frac{1}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)}\left((m+2)^{2}(m-2)^{2}-((m-4)^{2}+4(m-1))\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)}\left(m^{3}-5m^{2}+4\right)=\frac{4(m-1)}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)}\left((m-2)^{2}-8\right)>0

for all
m\geq 5
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}f(4n(n+m-2))=f(0)=\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}+4)\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+\alpha}

(7.16)

if the conformal class is large enough. We have

\displaystyle\frac{(m+2)^{2}(m-2)^{2}+2(m+2)^{2}\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)+\alpha}-\frac{m^{2}(m-4)^{2}+2((m-2)^{2}+4)\alpha+\alpha^{2}}{(m-4)^{2}+\alpha}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{((m-4)^{2}+\alpha)((m-4)^{2}+4(m-1)+\alpha)}\bigg\{4(m-1)(m-4)^{2}((m-2)^{2}-8)

\displaystyle+8(m^{3}-11m^{2}+24m-6)\alpha-4(3m-1)\alpha^{2}\bigg\}>0

if and only if

\displaystyle\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3m-1}\left(m^{3}-11m^{2}+24m-6+\sqrt{(m^{3}-11m^{2}+24m-6)^{2}+(m-1)(3m-1)(m-4)^{2}\left((m-2)^{2}-8\right)}\right).

Therefore, if

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\frac{4\pi(3m-1)}{m^{3}-11m^{2}+24m-6+\sqrt{(m^{3}-11m^{2}+24m-6)^{2}+(m-1)(3m-1)(m-4)^{2}\left((m-2)^{2}-8\right)}},

then (
7.16
) holds.
∎
